# Decisions

A decision recorded here is settled. To change one, add a new entry that
supersedes it and update the affected documents in the same change.

"Owner" means the project owner chose it. "Design" means it was chosen during
design and can be challenged with evidence.

| # | Decision | By | Why | Consequence |
|---|----------|----|-----|-------------|
| D-001 | The engine is written in Rust. | Owner | Performance, control over memory layout, and a compiler that catches most cross-module mistakes in a large codebase written by many agents. | Slower builds; no garbage collector to lean on. |
| D-002 | The primary frontend is a glyph grid in its own window. A terminal frontend exists with reduced effects. | Owner | Cutscenes need shake, zoom, slow motion and sub-cell motion. | The simulation outputs an abstract glyph scene; frontends are thin. |
| D-003 | Time is one shared timeline. Actions have phases with durations. The game pauses for decisions. | Owner | Speed, reactions, interrupts and clashes come from one mechanism. | No "turns" anywhere in engine code. |
| D-004 | The power ceiling is planet-buster. Nearby destruction is per cell; distant destruction is coarse and realised on arrival. | Owner | The fiction demands it, and the coarse layer is needed for off-screen events anyway. | Stamps and devastation (02 §17). Magnitudes span about 33 orders. |
| D-005 | The world away from the player runs as a layered, abstract simulation. | Owner | A world that moves without the player, at bounded cost. | Hot, warm and cold levels; promotion and demotion of characters. |
| D-006 | One z-level is one storey of 3 m. Moving things also carry an exact height. | Owner | Maps stay authorable and readable; motion stays smooth. | Cells are 1 × 1 × 3 m. Collapse and craters are storey-grained. |
| D-007 | Bodies are anatomical and data-defined. | Owner | Injuries, severing and executions are simulated outcomes. | Tissue shares the material table and damage rule with terrain. |
| D-008 | Modding is JSON and EOC first. A scripting hook is reserved; nothing depends on it. | Owner | Deterministic, safe, reloadable, and familiar from CDDA. | The EOC verb list must be broad enough to hold all of the bundled content's rules. |
| D-009 | The simulation is deterministic. | Design | Replays, golden tests, reproducible bugs, cutscenes that cannot disagree with outcomes, a path to lockstep co-op. | The rules in 01 §4, enforced by lints and tests. |
| D-010 | A tick is one microsecond, stored as `i64`. | Design | Fine enough for characters thousands of times faster than human; range of 292,000 years. | Durations in data are converted at load. |
| D-011 | Scheduled work belongs to clocks arranged in a tree, each with a rate. | Design | Time stop, slow fields and time-dilated realms become one feature. | The timeline must reschedule on rate change (F3). |
| D-012 | Space and time are integers. Physical magnitudes are `Real`, a deterministic `f64`, in SI units. | Design | Exact where exactness matters; wide range where it is needed; numbers a modder can look up. | No std transcendental functions; `libm` only. |
| D-013 | Chunk 32 × 32 × 8 cells; tract 32 × 32 column; region 64 × 64 tracts. | Design | Powers of two; tract footprint equals chunk footprint; a cell index fits in 13 bits. | Constants live in `dims` and nowhere else. |
| D-014 | A cell has three element layers (floor, block, fixture). An element's state is definition + material + bits, stored in per-chunk palettes. | Design | Independent destruction of floor and wall; any shape in any material; compact storage. | Natural ground has no floor layer; standing uses the solid top below. |
| D-015 | One material table serves terrain, items and bodies. | Design | One damage rule everywhere. | Material definitions carry properties for all three uses. |
| D-016 | Cells change only through `WorldEdit`, which records cause and emits events. | Design | Caches, saves, events and attribution cannot drift. | Chunk mutators are crate-private. |
| D-017 | `clash-world` never calls into the simulation or EOC. It emits events. | Design | The world can be built, tested and reasoned about alone. | No veto hooks from data in the world; use flags and definitions. |
| D-018 | Tracts are loaded on demand by interest tickets. There is no fixed bubble. | Design | Fights range over kilometres; processes must be able to finish. | A loaded-tract budget with force-resolve as the pressure valve. |
| D-019 | Changed tracts are stored in full. Unchanged tracts are regenerated. | Design | Robust to generator changes where it matters; small saves. | Pristine tracts follow mod updates. Configurable. |
| D-020 | Damage is a packet of energy and intensity against a threshold and a capacity. | Design | Distinguishes many weak hits from one strong one; conserves energy through obstacles; scales without special cases. | Callers must supply intensity. |
| D-021 | Blast intensity is released energy over swept volume, `E / (4/3·π·r³)`. | Design | Matches cube-root scaling of real explosions from grenades to impacts with one formula. | Thresholds for pressure waves are in pascals. |
| D-022 | Structural support is span propagation per storey, bottom up. No stress analysis. | Design | Cheap, deterministic, good enough to make collapse emergent. | No load limits initially (02 §26). |
| D-023 | Bodies in flight are point masses with a cross-section. No rotation. | Design | Legible, fast, deterministic. | Tumbling is presentation only. |
| D-024 | A stamp is stored once at the coarsest level containing its footprint and applied lazily at load. | Design | Planet-scale events cost one record. | Tracts track a stamp watermark. |
| D-025 | Subgrids are the single abstraction for moving pieces of world. | Design | Vehicles, ships, elevators and torn-off buildings share one mechanism. | Every position is a `Location` with a `GridId` from the start. |
| D-026 | Ambient conditions vary by altitude within a realm. | Design | Ground to orbit without changing realm. | Zero gravity in orbit is by declaration, not orbital mechanics. |
| D-027 | Planes are interaction masks on occupants. | Design | Astral and mirror layers without duplicating space. | Terrain exists on all planes. |
| D-028 | All bundled content is mods. Engine code names no content. | Design | Everything is replaceable; the modding surface is proven by use. | Fills, rubble and placeholders are named in data. |
| D-029 | Definition ids are namespaced `mod:name`. | Design | Mods cannot collide by accident; overrides are explicit. | Reference resolution rules in 01 §7. |
| D-030 | Entities use a purpose-built component store, not a third-party ECS. | Design, provisional | Deterministic order, controlled serialisation, reflective access, API stability. | Revisit in document 06 with a prototype. |
| D-031 | Tract storage is behind a trait; the default backend is `redb`. | Design, provisional | Atomic, incremental saves without designing a file format. | Replaceable if measurements say so. |
| D-032 | Documents are the source of truth. Code that must differ changes the document in the same change set. | Design | Many agents, no shared memory. | Reviews check documents and code together. |
| D-033 | CLASH2D is a genre-neutral engine kit: an ASCII immersive sim and superpower sandbox. Bundled content is small reference mods, not a flagship game. | Owner | The engine provides tools; creators provide results. | Rule R10. Supersedes the "first content mod is a modern city" note. Reference mods span several genres. |
| D-034 | The engine enforces no fairness. Nothing is scaled, softened or levelled. Authors balance their own content. | Owner | Ordinary people should be ordinary, and the gap to everything else should be felt. | No difficulty scaling, level scaling or damage caps in engine code. |
| D-035 | When a reaction window opens, and whether the player is asked, is decided by data-defined rules. | Owner | Different content wants different rhythms. | Document 03 defines the rule hooks and player-adjustable defaults. |
| D-036 | Zero, one or several controlled characters. Party control is optional and enabled per scenario. | Owner | Party play should be possible without being imposed. | `advance` returns decision requests that name the entity. |
| D-037 | Every action resolves from defaults and can also be fully specified. | Owner | Quick play and granular play are both wanted. | Document 07 must define defaults for every choice it exposes. |
| D-038 | Progression schemes are data. The engine supplies attributes, pools, counters and events. | Owner | Use-based skills, training, point-buy, levels and cheat skills should all be buildable and mixable. | No built-in experience or level system. |
| D-039 | All UI is built from a data-defined glyph toolkit, including the engine's own screens and main menu. | Owner | Megamods and total conversions need their own menus and screens. | Document 12 specifies the toolkit; bundled screens ship as a mod. |
| D-040 | Hitting many weak foes at once is an ability defined in data, not an engine shortcut. | Owner | Keeps one ruleset. | No "mook" concept in the engine. |
| D-041 | The world records truth; characters act on beliefs formed by perception. | Design | Stealth, disguise, deception, framing and manipulation need the two to differ. | Rule R11. AI may not read ground truth it has not perceived. |
| D-042 | Minds and bodies are separate and joined by a relation. Entities can contain entities. Instances are editable at runtime. | Design | Possession, raised corpses, soul transfer, mounts, hiding places and body-reshaping abilities become rearrangements of data. | Fixed in 01 §6 for document 06. |
| D-043 | Interactive props are ordinary elements that promote to device entities on demand. | Design | A world full of usable things must cost little until used. | 02 §5.5; document 16. |
| D-044 | World size and shape are per-realm data. There is no default planet. | Design | The owner had no preference, and the engine should not have one either. | Reference mods use small realms. |
| D-045 | Actions occupy channels. An entity may run several actions at once if their channel slots do not overlap. | Design | Walking while shooting, sustaining while fighting, and losing options with a lost limb all follow from one rule. | Bodies supply slots (document 06). |
| D-046 | There are two ways to be fast: tempo divides an action's phase times; a personal clock rate speeds everything about an entity. | Design | Skill and superhuman speed are different things and content should choose. | Both exist from the first build (03 §3.5). |
| D-047 | A reaction requires noticing. A window is noticed after a data-defined delay on the candidate's own clock, or not at all. | Design | Makes "too fast to react to" a fact of arithmetic. Implements D-034 in the timing system. | The engine does nothing by default when a window is noticed. |
| D-048 | Prompts, automatic reactions and plan interruptions are ordinary rules on window and perception events. | Design | Implements D-035 without a separate mechanism. | Bundled content ships the default rules. |
| D-049 | Entities hold plans. A decision is requested when a foreground action ends with an empty plan, or a rule interrupts. | Design | Long activities without constant prompting; AI and players share one structure. | 03 §5. |
| D-050 | A joint action is one action instance with several participants. | Design | Takedowns, grapples, carrying, mounting and group rituals need participants locked to one timeline. | 03 §8. |
| D-051 | Inheritance, patching and overriding operate on JSON trees before typed loading. | Design | One generic implementation for every definition type; exact source positions survive merging. | Typed loading uses a deserialiser over the spanned tree. |
| D-052 | Bare references resolve in the mod's own namespace, then its imports. Supersedes the "then core" wording first written in 01 §7. | Design | No mod is special, so total conversions need no special case. | `imports` defaults to `requires`. |
| D-053 | Hot reload is an in-memory save, a definition swap and a load. | Design | Reuses the path that save transparency already tests; no reload-specific code in subsystems. | Reload cost is proportional to loaded state. |
| D-054 | A profile is a named mod list with a boot screen. The engine runs with zero mods; placeholders live in the reserved `engine` namespace. | Design | Megamods and total conversions start as their own game. | 04 §3.3, §3.5. |
| D-055 | Settings that rules can read are simulation state, changed only by command. | Design | Keeps replays exact. | Display preferences are separate (document 12). |
| D-056 | Expressions are strings in a small infix language, compiled at load. Conditions may also be written as structures. | Design | Readable arithmetic; errors at load with positions. | Crate `clash-expr`. |
| D-057 | An effect is an object with one verb key. A rule with parameters can be used as a verb. | Design | Regular, schema-friendly, and lets mods build their own vocabulary. | Engine verbs take precedence over rule ids. |
| D-058 | Events are notifications (queued, after the fact) or hooks (immediate, alterable, cancellable). Hooks exist only for simulation-layer operations. | Design | Lets content reach into outcomes while keeping the world crate free of calls upward (D-017). | 05 §8.2. |
| D-059 | Rules are global or attached to an owner. Attached rules run only for events their owner takes part in. Recurring rules run on the owner's clock with a declared policy while unloaded. | Design | Thousands of rules cost nothing until relevant. | 05 §9. |
| D-060 | Variables are declared, typed and defaulted. Undeclared use is a warning, or an error under `strict_vars`. | Design | Misspelt variable names are the commonest bug in systems of this kind. | 05 §6. |
| D-061 | Every event, verb, property and function is registered with types and documentation; schemas and the modding reference are generated from the registries. | Design | The reference cannot drift from the engine. | Undocumented registrations fail tests. |
| D-062 | The kernel entity (place, tags, variables, control, clock, channels, plan, attachments) is fixed in 03 and extended by document 06. | Design | Lets time, rules and actions be built before the full entity model. | Document 06 may add, not remove. |
| D-063 | New dependencies: `schemars` for schema generation; `notify` for file watching in tools only. | Design | Standard, well-maintained crates for solved problems. | `notify` must never be a dependency of a simulation crate. |
| D-064 | Component types are a fixed engine set. Mods extend entities through variables, attributes, traits and rules. | Design | Full tooling, saving and type checking for everything content adds. | 06 §3. |
| D-065 | Three numeric primitives: attributes (base plus modifiers), pools (bounded, with rates), tracks (accumulating, with levels). | Design | Implements D-038: every progression scheme is built from them. | Pools and stocks are analytic between changes; nothing runs per tick. |
| D-066 | One concept, the trait, covers statuses, mutations, perks, blessings and curses. | Design | Fewer concepts; a status is a trait with a duration. | 06 §5. |
| D-067 | Bodies are trees of parts built of material layers. Hits resolve through the layers by the world's packet rule. There are no hit points. | Design | Implements D-007 and D-034 with the rule that already breaks walls. | Armour is worn layers. Toughness is material. |
| D-068 | Body functions, and stopping (`die`), are mechanisms. When a body stops is content. | Design | Undead, constructs and things that refuse to die need no special cases. | Bundled content supplies the usual rule. |
| D-069 | A mind is a component that can move between entities. A driver acts for a body without moving a mind. | Design | Implements D-042. | 06 §8. |
| D-070 | Characters are built from templates with choices. "Species" is a kind of template. Choices range from picking options to free part-by-part assembly. | Owner default | The owner asked for great expressive room; no preference between authored and assembled was given, so both. | Creation screens are driven by template data. |
| D-071 | Individuals are anonymous and regenerated until something makes them notable, after which they are stored. | Design | Implements D-005 at the level of people. | 06 §12. |
| D-072 | Perception is signals reaching senses. Detection is an awareness level evaluated on events, never every tick. | Design | Stealth follows from numbers; cost follows activity. | 15 §3–§5. |
| D-073 | Tracks, memories and relations refer to personas, not entities. | Design | Implements D-041: disguise, secret identities and framing need no special cases. | 15 §8. |
| D-074 | Static geometry is common knowledge to AI. Dynamic facts are never free. | Design | Full map memory for every character costs too much for what it adds. | Elements tagged `hidden` are excluded until perceived. |
| D-075 | There is no dialogue-tree system. Speech is messages and lines chosen by rules; talking raises a hook that rules fill with options. | Owner | Emergent reactions over authored trees. | A mod can still build trees from `ask`. |
| D-076 | Presentation keeps an event-sourced view model, plays events back over real time, and shows only what the party perceived. | Design | Needed for playback and cutscenes; implements R11 for the player. | 12 §5, §7. |
| D-077 | A frame is a glyph grid plus free sprites. Nothing essential may depend on what a terminal cannot draw. | Design | Implements D-002. | The log is complete. |
| D-078 | Cutscene triggers are evaluated in the simulation and emit cues. Choreography cannot alter, drop or reorder what happened. | Design | Triggers need simulation state at the moment of the event; outcomes must not depend on presentation. | 12 §8. |
| D-079 | Controls are content. Any action can be bound twice: quick, from defaults, and precise, asking for every parameter. | Design | Implements D-037 at the keyboard. | The engine has no built-in move or attack key. |
| D-080 | Ordinary screens see non-party entities only as perceived. Debug screens see truth. | Design | The interface must not leak what the character does not know. | Enforced at load by binding context. |
| D-081 | The window frontend supports the mouse. Everything remains reachable by keyboard. | Owner default | No preference given. | The terminal frontend is keyboard-first. |
| D-082 | Three vehicle forms: mount (a creature), craft (a device entity), vessel (a subgrid with a hull entity). | Design | Each maps onto something that already exists. | 11 §3. |
| D-083 | Vehicle performance is derived from parts, mass and medium. One resource bus per vehicle by default. | Design | Damage and load degrade vehicles without special rules. | Finer wiring in document 16. |
| D-084 | Every cell has a medium: gas, liquid or vacuum, with density and pressure. The sea is still liquid; waves are not simulated. | Design | Buoyancy, drag, depth pressure, underwater light and sound from one query. | 11 §4. |
| D-085 | Sealed volumes hold compartments that exchange gas and liquid through openings by pressure difference. Hulls fail under pressure by the ordinary damage rule. | Design | Flooding, air pockets, ballast, decompression and crush depth from one mechanism, for sea and space alike. | 11 §10. |
| D-086 | Vehicles move in any direction. A vessel's grid swings in quarter-turns. | Design | Exact interiors without shear. Settles 02 §26 item 3. | A ship heading diagonally is drawn at the nearest quarter. |
| D-087 | Travel bands are data. | Design | Sea, undersea, underground and whatever else content needs. | Supersedes the fixed list first written in 02 §20.1. |
| D-088 | New dependencies for the window frontend: `winit`, `wgpu`, `fontdue`. | Design | Standard choices. | Confined to `clash-front-window`. |
| D-089 | Survival, base building and automation are first-class uses of the engine, alongside gadgets and powered suits. | Owner | Inspirations: CDDA bases, RimWorld, Minecraft tech and magic mods; a superpower setting needs its equipment. | Document 16; scenes S24 and S25. |
| D-090 | Resources are data, each with a quantity and a rate. One network evaluator balances them for world grids, vehicles, suits and gadgets alike. | Design | Custom grids of any kind; one implementation to test. Supersedes the flow and stock kinds first written in 11 §6.2. | 16 §5. |
| D-091 | A network balances rates, sheds consumers by priority, and treats stores as straight lines between changes. | Design | Devices need real supply; nothing runs per tick. | Shortage propagates through converters. |
| D-092 | Conduits are a fourth, sparse cell layer hosted by floors and blocks. | Design | Wiring must not occupy the fixture or block of a cell. | 02 §5.1, §6.2 amended. |
| D-093 | Elements may be passive loads counted by their network without becoming entities. City-scale utilities are tract services, not wiring. | Design | A lit building or a powered city must cost almost nothing. | 16 §4.3, §4.4. |
| D-094 | Signals are values on networks. Logic is built from devices with expressions; the engine has no gate primitives. | Design | Mechanism, not meaning. | Bundled content supplies a basic set. |
| D-095 | Crafting, machine processing, growing and construction are one definition type: the recipe. Work is done at a rate and completion is a single scheduled event. | Design | One concept for modders; no per-tick machines. | 16 §8, §10. |
| D-096 | Unfinished work is a job with stored progress that anyone may continue. The job list is the interface to the AI layer. | Design | Base building by player and helpers; nothing lost to interruption. | Document 09 assigns work. |
| D-097 | An assembly is a device with module slots sharing a bus. Gadgets, powered suits, implants and vehicles are assemblies. What modules confer lasts only while they are supplied and intact. | Design | One mechanism for all equipment. | 16 §11; 11 §6 aligned. |
| D-098 | A sealed suit is a one-compartment sealed volume around its wearer. | Design | Life support, breaches and crushing reuse the hull rules. | 16 §11.4; 11 §10. |
| D-099 | Machines left behind catch up by running their own event logic on a private timeline with batching, or stay loaded by holding a ticket. | Design | Correct off-screen production without simulating the place. | 16 §13. |
| D-100 | Every interaction is a definition whose effects are rules. The engine has no built-in "open" or "use". | Design | Mechanism, not meaning. | 16 §3. |
| D-101 | Combat adds no timing system and no damage system. An attack is a block on an action and ends in a hit on a body or a packet on an element. | Design | One ruleset; everything in 03, 06 and 02 applies to fighting unchanged. | 07 §1, §3. |
| D-102 | Chance enters combat only through data-defined contests and shot dispersion. | Design | Content chooses how random fighting is, down to not at all. | 07 §4.1. |
| D-103 | A strike's intensity is its energy over the striking surface's area times its give. Surfaces also carry a span, which decides severing. | Design | Edge, point and fist differ for physical reasons; the same form as body impacts. | 07 §3.2, §6.1; 06 amended. |
| D-104 | A defence either avoids or interposes. What it can turn aside is capped by what the defender can hold. Nothing defends automatically. | Design | Implements D-034 in defence: skill does not stop a blow far beyond one's strength. | 07 §5. |
| D-105 | Energy a strike delivers that the target neither absorbs nor passes returns into the striking surface. | Design | Strength without durability harms its user; weapons wear on hard targets. | 07 §6.3. |
| D-106 | An authored share of a strike's energy pushes the target. Speed follows from mass; below thresholds nothing or a stagger, above them flight as a world body. | Design | Knockback at every scale from one formula. | 07 §7. |
| D-107 | Strikes aimed at each other and due within a window clash and are resolved once: near-equal powers lock and spill energy into the world, otherwise the stronger carries through at the difference. | Design | The engine's namesake. Scene S5. | 07 §8. |
| D-108 | A multi-target attack carries the energy that passed each target on to the next. | Design | Implements D-040: cutting down a rank is a consequence of energy and edge, and stops at whoever can stop it. | 07 §6.4. |
| D-109 | Takedowns and executions are joint actions with a forced role and strikes that cannot miss, still delivered through the body model. | Design | Choreographed and certain to connect, but not certain to kill. | 07 §10. |
| D-110 | Projectiles are bodies in flight with a striking surface. Instant shots by ray serve beams and reduced detail. | Design | Cover, penetration, arc and time of flight from the world's rules; fast characters can respond, others cannot. | 07 §11. |
| D-111 | Every strike takes an effort. Previews are pure and use only what the viewer could know. | Design | Holding back, informed precise targeting, and AI that can be surprised. Implements D-037 and R11 in combat. | 07 §3.3, §13. |
| D-112 | Stances, styles, combinations, feints and counters are content built from traits, branching phases and reactions. | Design | Mechanism, not meaning. | 07 §12; traits gain exclusive groups. |
| D-113 | An ability is one definition type for skills, spells, powers and gifts alike: a bundle of grants, passive effects, modes, a rank and parameters. The engine does not distinguish kinds. | Design | Mechanism, not meaning. A sword style and a planet-cracking beam are the same structure. | 08 §3. |
| D-114 | Abilities are conferred by templates, traits, equipment or rules, and are one instance across sources. Innate and equipped abilities are the same thing. | Design | A suit's repulsors and a mutant's eye beams should not need different systems. | 08 §3.3. |
| D-115 | Ability parameters are modifiable values, like attributes. | Design | One definition serves every rank; items, moods and other abilities can strengthen or weaken a power. | 08 §4.2. |
| D-116 | Costs are taken from pools, stores or items when an action starts, in full or not at all. A cost may allow overdraw from another pool. | Design | Pushing past limits at a price is a common story and should be one field. | 08 §5.2. |
| D-117 | Sustained abilities are modes with an upkeep that end when their pool empties. Concentration, gesture and voice are channels. | Design | No per-tick upkeep; interruption and silencing need no special cases. | 08 §5.3, §5.4. |
| D-118 | Reactive expressions: pure expressions re-evaluated only when something they read changes. | Design | Conditions such as "while not under water" without polling. Shared with device outputs. | 05 §7.5. |
| D-119 | Abilities go dormant by their own condition and are suppressed from outside by tag. Either confers nothing until it clears. | Design | Anti-magic, dampeners, weaknesses and requirements from one mechanism. | 08 §6. |
| D-120 | Areas may be anchored to an entity and may override gravity inside them. Light sources may be negative. | Design | Auras, carried fields, time bubbles, gravity wells and darkness. | 02 §8.3 amended. |
| D-121 | What an ability leaves in the world is bound to it and ends with it, by removal, restoration or release. | Design | Curses, summons and conjured matter clean up after themselves, or do not, by declaration. | 08 §7.3. |
| D-122 | Snapshots of an entity or a bounded box of cells can be taken and restored. They are local, capped and owned by an ability. | Design | Recall, mending and turning back a room. Closes the rewinding question of 03 for local cases. | 08 §7.4. Whole-world return is left to scenarios. |
| D-123 | Truth reaches a character only by being recorded as knowledge: `appraise` stores true values in that character's track, and screens may show those. | Design | Revealing abilities without breaking R11. | 08 §7.5; 15 amended. |
| D-124 | The engine supplies no protection from one's own power. Durability, control and immunity are things content gives on purpose. | Design | Implements D-034 for abilities; recoil and unowned releases already ensure it. | 08 §10. |
| D-125 | A mind is a set of goals, each scored by an expression. The highest is pursued through methods whose steps become the character's plan. | Design | Data-defined, explainable, and it produces the same actions a player would issue. | 09 §3, §4. |
| D-126 | Goals, methods and tactics compile in a mind context that can read only the character's own state, its beliefs and perceptions, and static geometry. Anything else is a load error. | Design | Rule R11 enforced by the compiler instead of by convention. | 09 §3.4. |
| D-127 | Moment-to-moment choices are made by scoring every available action against a purpose. Harm is judged by preview; control, escape and aid by the ability's own value function. | Design | Characters use trickery and flight on the same footing as blows. | 09 §6.1. |
| D-128 | A character estimates threat from what it has seen or been told another can do, and its mind may act on being outmatched. | Design | Implements D-034 from the weaker side: ordinary people should know to run. | 09 §6.2. |
| D-129 | Navigation is hierarchical over tracts, per-chunk regions and cells, rebuilt lazily when geometry changes. Many movers to one place share a flow field. | Design | Paths must survive a destructible world and scale to formations. | 09 §5. |
| D-130 | A group may have a collective that decides for it and issues orders. Orders must be heard. Formations are data. | Design | Command, and its loss, from mechanisms that already exist. | 09 §8. |
| D-131 | Warm characters are advanced in bulk and exchange harm by expected values from previews. | Design | Scene S19 at affordable cost. | 09 §9.1. Believability to be tested. |
| D-132 | Unloaded notable characters are agents with routines and long goals, costing nothing between wake-ups. What a character was doing survives unloading as an aim. | Design | A world that moves without the player, and characters who do not forget what they were about. | 09 §10. |
| D-133 | An incident has a cast and stages. Each stage has a live form (goals for real characters) and an abstract form (effects on records). Exactly one applies, and an incident can change between them midway. | Design | Things happen for reasons whether or not anyone is watching, and can be walked in on. | 09 §11. |
| D-134 | Incidents occur by rate (scheduled, never polled) or because someone started one. | Design | Ambient events and purposeful ones share a definition. | 09 §11.2. |
| D-135 | Knowledge lives in minds, groups, places (local knowledge) and channels, and moves between them by rules with delay and loss. | Design | Witnesses out of crowds, wanted lists, news, and investigations from the gap between record and recollection. | 09 §12. |
| D-136 | Site stocks, shipments, services, weather and calendars are cold-layer mechanisms with no meaning assigned by the engine. | Design | Trade, blackouts and storms for mods that want them, at no cost to those that do not. | 09 §13, §14. |
| D-137 | Every choice a character makes can be opened and read: goal scores, plan, options considered, route. | Design | A character that does something inexplicable is a content bug that must be findable. | 09 §16. |
| D-138 | Places are authored as maps: rows of characters per storey with a palette of cell specs. Maps take parameters, contain other maps, and turn and mirror. | Design | Familiar from CDDA, readable, diffable, and enough to draw anything on a storey grid. | 10 §3. |
| D-139 | Randomness in generation comes only from positional seeds, through a generation context for expressions. | Design | Keeps generation pure and independent of when or in what order it runs. | 10 §3.4. |
| D-140 | Maps reach the world through placements, which are records. A map may span many tracts; each realises its window. | Design | Buildings larger and smaller than a tract; surveys stay cheap. | 10 §4; 02 §9.1 amended. |
| D-141 | The same maps are applied to the live world, queued as pending edits, and used as construction designs. | Design | One format for generation, aftermath, conjuring, set dressing and blueprints. | 10 §4.3. Replaces the "mapgen update" wording of 02. |
| D-142 | Ground is a list of native steps configured by data. Whether a stratum anchors is chosen per stratum. | Design | Settles 02 §26 item 2: rock holds, loose ground can cave in. | 10 §5. |
| D-143 | Large places are built from parts: stacks of storey maps with a shared core, and assemblies of pieces joined at joints. | Design | A tower from five maps; dungeons, stations and sewers from rooms. | 10 §7. |
| D-144 | Where things go is decided top down in three steps that write only records: a realm plan once, a region survey on first need, and tract realisation. | Design | Global consistency without ever generating the globe. | 10 §2, §8, §9. |
| D-145 | Neighbouring regions agree by drawing planned lines from the realm plan and deriving minor crossings from a hash of the shared edge. | Design | Regions can be surveyed in any order and still meet. | 10 §8.3. |
| D-146 | A world may be authored, generated or mixed, and realms may be created on demand when a chart node is first reached. | Design | Scenario arenas, hand-made castles on generated islands, endless dungeons and galaxies from one mechanism. | 10 §9.3, §9.4. |
| D-147 | Ruin, abandonment and twisted copies are transforms applied after realisation. | Design | One mechanism for devastated tracts, derived realms and local variation. | 10 §10. |
| D-148 | Generation seeds the simulation: maps mark named places, site definitions name staff, owners and stocks, populations have homes and a presence by time of day. | Design | The layered simulation has something to run on from the first moment. | 10 §11. |
| D-149 | A scenario is a definition: worlds, characters or how to make them, a start, rules, and what ends it. Modes (sandbox, roguelike, campaign, skirmish, survival, investigation) are combinations of its fields, not engine features. | Design | Mechanism, not meaning. | 13 §3, §11. |
| D-150 | The player may start by taking over any notable inhabitant of the world. | Design | Every notable is a full character; being an ordinary person in an indifferent world should be one line. | 13 §4.2. |
| D-151 | Missions are templates for objectives and can be carried by published knowledge as postings, which spread and go stale. | Design | Missions come from the world and its people instead of from a menu. | 13 §5.2. |
| D-152 | The director paces incidents near the player by a tension value with lulls. It decides when, where and what kind. Bundled directors never weigh by the party's strength. | Design | Implements D-034 for pacing: rhythm, not difficulty matching. | 13 §7. An author can write otherwise. |
| D-153 | A case joins something that happened, the true record of it, and what each holder has found. Accusations are judged by the holder's rules and are not checked against the truth. | Design | Investigation, wrongful conviction and being wanted from one structure. Implements D-041 for consequences. | 13 §8. |
| D-154 | Saving, restoring and ending are requests from rules that the application carries out at safe points. Saving policy is a scenario field. | Design | Permadeath, checkpoints and free saving without engine modes. | 13 §9; 03 and 01 amended. |
| D-155 | A restore may carry the controlled characters' memories across. | Design | Return after death with foreknowledge, by configuration. Completes D-122. | 13 §9.3. |
| D-156 | Legacy variables belong to the player's profile and are copied into the simulation at start and load. Kept characters form a roster. Scenarios chain into campaigns with a carry list. | Design | What persists between games, without breaking replay. | 13 §10. |
| D-157 | "Scenario" names two things: the definition type of document 13, and the scripted regression tests of the plans. The second is called a test script where it could be unclear. | Design | The collision was noticed late; renaming every plan was judged riskier than a clear note. | CLAUDE.md; 13 §1. |
| D-158 | A tool is a frontend. It reads through views and changes the simulation only through logged commands. | Design | No private way in means nothing a tool does can be unreproducible. | 14 §2. |
| D-159 | One console language serves the in-game console, the terminal viewer and test scripts. Every verb is a console command by registration. | Design | What can be typed can be scripted and tested. | 14 §3, §7. |
| D-160 | Everything the engine declines to do returns a reason: a code, a sentence and the values involved. | Design | The player, a character's mind and a modder all need to know why. A refusal without one is a defect. | 14 §5. |
| D-161 | The inspector's contents are generated from the property registry, with why-views per kind of subject and views of truth against belief. | Design | New properties appear without inspector work; disguise and stale belief are visible. | 14 §4. |
| D-162 | One trace system for every subsystem, free when off. | Design | Explaining behaviour must not change it. | 14 §6.1. |
| D-163 | Every budget row of every specification is collected in one file and checked by one gate. | Design | Targets become measurements in one place. Replaces the separate gates of each plan. | 14 §6.3. |
| D-164 | Every invariant in every specification has a run-time checker or a named test. | Design | Invariants are only worth stating if they are checked. | 14 §7.3. |
| D-165 | A save and its input log replay to logged hashes; a mismatch is located by interval and subsystem. Development builds can rewind by snapshot and replay. | Design | Nondeterminism and bugs seen once must be findable. Rewind here is a tool, distinct from D-122 and D-155. | 14 §8. |
| D-166 | The map editor is a screen built with the UI toolkit that reads and writes the map format. Anything built in play can be captured as a map. | Design | Maps are grids of characters and so is the screen. Answers the authoring risk of 10 §16. | 14 §10. |
| D-167 | The engine balances nothing, so it shows authors what they made: a matchup report from previews, and a compatibility report for a set of mods. | Design | The burden of balance (D-034) needs instruments. | 14 §9.3, §9.4. |
| D-168 | An incident's live and abstract forms are compared by running both many times. | Design | Answers the agreement risk of 09 §20. | 14 §11. |
| D-169 | Load-bearing blocks have a limit: the load they carry against their material's bearing strength, reduced by damage. A crushed block is destroyed and the check repeats. | Owner | Damaged supports must be able to fail (scene S2), and what players build must obey physics. Replaces the "no load limits" consequence of D-022. | 02 §14.5. Weight uses local gravity, so heavy-gravity areas can crush and nothing is crushed weightless. |
| D-170 | Generation leaves nothing unsupported. Caves are carved only in strata that anchor. | Owner | A generated cave in loose ground would fall at the first nearby edit. | 10 §5.2; invariant X9. |
| D-171 | A creature meant to be stood on, climbed or entered is a subgrid whose cells are its body. Anything else is an occupant filling one or several cells. The line is use, not size. | Owner | Climbing, riding and cutting into a titan reuse what vessels already do. | 06 §6.6. Such a body is rigid: its limbs do not move relative to it. |
| D-172 | Sight, rays and signals cross subgrid boundaries without limit. They pass a portal only if it allows it, only for a viewer within its range, and one portal deep by default. | Owner | Seeing out of a vehicle is not optional; seeing through portals costs keeping the far side loaded. | 02 §22; 12 §6.1. |
| D-173 | An area may keep a journal of the cell changes made inside it for a span, and `rewind_area` undoes them. Minds are not rewound. | Owner | Undoing the last moments in a place without having marked a moment first. Extends D-122. | 08 §7.4; 02 §8.3. Costs memory only where things change. |
| D-174 | `restore_as_generated` writes a place back as generation made it. | Owner | Generation is pure, so repair needs no snapshot. | 08 §7.4. It removes what was built since. |
| D-175 | The number of characters simulated in full is capped (`max_hot_minds`, default 150). Beyond the cap, characters in hot tracts are stepped as warm, lowest attention first: controlled and ticket-holding, then those involved with them, then notable by distance, then anonymous by distance. Physics has no such levels. | Design, delegated by the owner | Tract levels are by distance, and a battlefield fits inside the hot radius, which contradicted 09's account of large battles. | 09 §9.3; 03 §10. |
| D-176 | Crowds do not jam: a mover waits behind one going the same way, swaps with one who yields, and gives up after no progress. Warm characters do not block each other. | Design, delegated by the owner | One blocking creature per cell would deadlock formations at doorways. | 09 §5.5; 06 §11. |
| D-177 | `map check` realises a map and runs the support and load passes. A map that does not stand as drawn is reported. | Owner | With load limits, an authored building could collapse on first load. | 10 §3.7. |
| D-178 | A vessel is drawn at its true heading in views zoomed out far enough to show it as an outline. | Owner | Softens the quarter-turn look of D-086 where it shows most. | 12 §6.1; 11 §5.5. |
| D-179 | An area rewind may also give the characters inside it back the beliefs, memories and relations they had, so they forget the undone span. It reaches no further: watchers outside, anyone told, group knowledge and local knowledge keep what they have. | Owner | Leaving minds alone is the only default that never contradicts itself; letting those inside forget is a reasonable, cheap power whose limits are clear. | 08 §7.4. |
| D-180 | A rule, including an ability's, may turn the whole world back by a span: continuous snapshots while history is kept, replay to the exact tick, then memories carried for those exempt. It is independent of saving policy and is stated to be costly. | Owner | A true rewind for authors who need everyone everywhere to have forgotten. Supersedes "not by an ability" in the earlier wording of 13 §1 and 08 §15. | 13 §9.5; scenarios package SC11; shares machinery with D-165. |
| D-181 | Whole-world rewind (D-180) is an experimental feature, and is marked so wherever it appears. Experimental features are a general marking: a mod must opt in by name, is warned at every load, and nothing bundled may use one. | Owner | The feature is wanted as an option for authors, but its cost is unmeasured and its interactions are not worked through. It must not be mistaken for something dependable. | 04 §8.4; 13 §9.5 with its list of unresolved questions; package SC11 is built last and no milestone depends on it. |
| D-182 | A window is offered to those it names, to anyone holding toward one of them a relation flagged `intervenes`, and to directly controlled characters, the last two within the window kind's `reach`. A reaction flagged `on_behalf` may answer for someone else. Warm characters have no windows. | Owner | A bodyguard or a party member must be able to step in. Bystanders who only witness still react through percepts, and warm detail stays cheap. | 03 §7.1, §16; 15 §9.1; 07 §5.1; package T5. |
| D-183 | Translation and single-file archives are deferred, with the direction for translation fixed. Reload stays whole. Two mods replacing one field with different values is a warning unless the later names the earlier in `requires` or `after`. | Owner | None of the deferred items changes anything else when it arrives. Silent last-wins patching is the commonest way mod sets go wrong. | 04 §6.3, §7.1, §20; package P2. |
| D-184 | Maps are a value type. A rule may `return` a value to a caller in an effect list. Rules are still not callable from expressions, and there are no lambdas. Rules may belong to groups that are switched on and off. | Owner | Large mods need tables, small computations and optional rule sets. Expressions must stay unable to change anything. | 05 §3, §9.5, §9.6, §20; packages X2, X4, K5, K6. |
| D-185 | Unchanged bodies of one template share their parts. A stack splits a member off before it changes and may take it back when it is equal again. The reference body keeps its present detail. | Owner | Crowds and piles of goods must be cheap without anything behaving differently. | 06 §6.4, §9.1, §16; packages E4, E8. |
| D-186 | Attacks have a reach in height. A swing strikes what stands in its arc. A defence may protect someone else. A grip may take hold of the world, to hang on or to wrench a piece away. Bodies in flight do not collide with each other: stopping a shot is a reaction, by interposing, catching or intercepting. Whether bulk battle looks right stays open until it can be tested. | Owner | Each closes a gap between what the scenes ask for and what the rules allowed, with mechanisms already present. | 07 §4.3, §5.1, §6.4, §9.3, §11.6, §19; packages C2, C3, C4, C7, C9. |
| D-187 | What fields do to each other, to tagged materials and to arriving damage is written as `field_reaction` definitions. Seeing the future is deferred until whole-world rewind has been tried. Scoring helpers are supplied for abilities that do no harm; how well characters use them is open until tested. | Owner | One table of reactions serves every element in every mod. Looking ahead needs the experimental machinery of D-181 and should not be designed before that has been measured. | 02 §16; 08 §15; 09 §6.1; packages W16, I5, A10. |
| D-188 | Minds stay reactive: no search-based planning. Only controlled characters keep map memory. A track keeps a bounded list of what its persona has been seen or said to do, and `threat` reads it. AI drivers park by berths with approach lines. Bulk exchanges assume each side's usual guard. Whether bulk fighting looks continuous with full fighting stays open until tested. | Owner | Each is the cheapest thing that makes the behaviour hold. Planning and getting lost are large costs for effects authors can write as methods. | 09 §5.6, §6.1, §6.2, §9.1, §20; 15 §7.1; packages I2, I5, I8, S5. |
| D-189 | Lakes are planned basins with their own water levels. Paths have kinds with crossings: bridges, tunnels, cuttings, embankments. A survey tests placements for overlap in three dimensions. A history for generated worlds is deferred; when taken up it will age the realm plan in coarse steps, not run the cold simulation for years. | Owner | Water above sea level, roads that cross things and cellars over sewers are needed by ordinary worlds. History is wanted and is not needed to start. | 10 §4.1, §5.1, §8.5, §9.2, §16; packages WG3, WG6, WG7. |
| D-190 | A vessel's list is a number with consequences up to capsizing; its grid stays upright. Waves are not simulated. A swing that is obstructed is refused unless forced as a ram. Fleets are collectives of vehicles in formation. A sea or lake is raised or lowered by `set_liquid_level`, a coarse event. | Owner | Ships that roll over, cannot turn in a creek and sail in company, at the cost of a number and a refusal. Completes D-086. | 11 §4.3, §5.5, §7, §10.6, §18; 02 §17.3; packages V1, V4, V6b, V7, W17. |
| D-191 | Several controlled characters may be given one command together. `show` definitions may name a sound cue that nothing plays in the first version. Tilesets are out of the first version. Wide characters, screen-reader support and controllers are deferred. | Owner | Party play and one mind in several bodies need it. The rest can be added without changing what exists; naming sound cues now lets content be written ahead. | 12 §7.3, §9.6, §16; packages U5, U7. |
| D-192 | A save is a world: shared worlds across saves are not provided. A closed case is judged again when its holder gains a finding. A director's distance may be travel time at the party's recent pace. Seeing ahead and cheaper world history are deferred with the experimental rewind. | Owner | Evidence that overturns a verdict is part of scene S15. A party that crosses a continent in a minute must still have things happen within its reach. | 13 §7.3, §8.5, §16; packages SC5, SC6. |
| D-193 | Stepping through a rule is offered by running it again from an in-memory save to a later effect, never by suspending the simulation. The map editor gains a sheet view for maps made of maps. Both are later additions. A remote inspector and translation tools are deferred. Distributing mods is out of scope. | Owner | Stepping without a resumable interpreter keeps rule R5 and costs little. The rest does not block anything. | 14 §6.4, §10.2, §18; packages TL10, TL15. |
| D-194 | Appearance is a set of content-defined features, each needing a clarity to make out. A witness holds a description, which may match several personas. Characters notice when a watched thing at their own site is not as they last saw it. What they conclude is for rules. Light has no colour yet. | Owner | "A tall figure in red" is what investigation and disguise turn on. Noticing the open door is the smallest piece of inference that everything else can be built from. | 15 §6, §8.4, §9.1, §18; 10 §3.2; packages S4, S6, S7, WG1. |
| D-195 | Conduits of different capacity are separate networks joined by a junction that passes no more than the smaller. A conduit may have a loss per metre, drawn as a standing load. A device may carry a logic table that its user fills in during play. Floors may convey what is on them. Staleness in catch-up across sites is accepted. | Owner | Thin wires, long runs, programmable controllers and belts that can be ridden are what automation is made of, each done without tracking flow along every segment. | 16 §5.5, §6.1, §6.2, §7, §9.4, §17; packages G4, G5, G7. |
| D-196 | The AI aims at consistency, not cleverness. Characters follow readable rules that depend on who they are, what they believe and how they feel, with some chance in them. They do not reason about the player, scheme or build deceptions over time, whatever powers they hold. Schemes are authored as plots, incidents or methods. | Owner | Planning around the player is too complicated to do well, and a character whose behaviour can be learned and relied on is what stealth, fear and immersive play are built from. | 00 §5, §6; 09 §1, §20; 08 §15; package I10. Closes the open question on how well characters use trickery. |
| D-197 | A generated world's history is not simulated. The barest level stands: places may be given a few facts from the seed, and transforms make them look their age. This replaces the deferral in D-189. | Owner | Not wanted. Worlds need to look lived in, not to have been lived in. | 00 §5, §6; 10 §16. |
| D-198 | A resource may be an account: held as balances by characters, groups and sites, moved by `pay` and by standing payments at a rate, and usable as a recipe input. Items may be tokens worth an amount. The engine gives accounts no meaning. | Owner | Wealth, wages, costs and income had no mechanism, and one example used a `pay` effect that did not exist. A balance with straight-line rates costs nothing while idle and serves money, favour and budgets alike. | 16 §5.6; 15 §9.2; 13 §5.2 example corrected; package G13. |
| D-199 | `trade` exchanges items, amounts, standing payments and sites between two parties, wholly or not at all. Offers are gathered by a hook. Goods not at hand are delivered as shipments. A site is held by a group or a character, and holding one earns only through its stock's rates and payments that content sets up. | Owner | Buying, selling, hiring, owning and ordering are one atomic exchange plus what the cold layer already moves. Deliveries travel through the world, so they can be waited for and intercepted. | 16 §3.6; 09 §13.4; 10 §11.1; 12 §10.7 (trade and fitting screens); 00 scene S26; packages G14, L5. |
| D-200 | An assembly that is an item may stand alone: the same entity is given a body made of its own layers, movement and a mind, and is then a character until it stands down or is put on. | Owner | A suit that comes when called, guards a room or fights in a rank of its fellows must be the same suit, with the same damage and stores, as the one that is worn. | 16 §11.6, invariant G13; package G15. |
| D-201 | A machine that fights with its own limbs is a driven body: a creature with a seat that makes its occupant the driver. It is distinct from a suit, which is worn, and a craft or vessel, which is operated from stations with mounted weapons. | Owner | A piloted machine that punches, grapples and loses limbs should use the whole of combat, which works on bodies. The pieces existed; the pattern and what the pilot's own body does were unwritten. | 11 §8.1; 06 §8.2; 16 §11.4; package V15. |
| D-202 | Temperature is derived, never simulated: the region's (a known function of time), adjusted by stratum, depth or vacuum, replaced inside a sealed volume by its held temperature, plus what warming and cooling fields in the cell add. A `climate` device moves a sealed volume's held temperature in a straight line toward a set point. | Owner | Cold and heat survival, heated shelters and freezing seas need one number per cell. Conducting heat through the world would cost far more than it is worth. | 11 §4.6; 02 §4.1; 09 §14.1; 16 §4.2; packages V1, V3, L6, G2. |
| D-203 | Materials and fields may change state by temperature: into another material, another element, a field, or by gaining a cover such as ice on water. Changes are tested only when a warming or cooling field changes in the cell, when the region's temperature crosses a threshold, and when a tract loads. Rubble may differ by damage type. A floor on denser still liquid floats and carries a limited mass. | Owner | Water that freezes, ice that melts and lava that cools were missing. Three test moments keep it free of polling. | 02 §5.4, §12.3, §14.1, §16, invariant W11; package W25. |
| D-204 | Devastation has a kind. `devastate` writes it directly for causes that are not blasts, with a transform per kind for places nobody was watching and ruin variants by kind. `tremor` lowers span and bearing over an extent for a time, so that what was only just standing falls. | Owner | Earthquakes, floods, fires and ashfall should feed the same aftermath as explosions: one number and one kind for the cold layer, and a mark on the land wherever the player later goes. | 02 §14.6, §17.2, §17.3, invariant W12; 09 §15; 10 §6, §10.2; packages W24, L7, WG5. |
| D-205 | A release may be shaped as a front: a wall of given width and height advancing in one direction, with intensity equal to energy over the volume swept, following the surface, sheltering behind what survives, pushing along its direction and optionally leaving a field. | Owner | Waves, avalanches and flows had no mechanism. The existing release already does everything but the shape. | 02 §13.5; package W24. |
| D-206 | The medium can be moving. Drift is the region's wind in the open, its current in the sea, a watercourse's current along its line, or what an area sets, and none under cover. It carries fields that opt in, bodies in flight, swimmers and floating things, and pushes occupants when strong enough. | Owner | Wind and current acted only on vehicles. Smoke, fire, thrown things, rivers, storms, whirlwinds and whirlpools all follow from one vector per cell. | 11 §4.7; 02 §8.3, §15.1, §16; 10 §8.5; packages V1, W7, W14, W16, WG7. |
| D-207 | A region keeps one number per lasting weather field, its cover, raised by weather and lowered by the field's own rule. Tracts are given it when they load. | Owner | Snow that fell while nobody was there should be lying when someone arrives. One number per region costs nothing. | 09 §14.1; package L6. |
| D-208 | The bundled interface gains make, build, work, device and know-how screens and map overlays for networks, areas, jobs, temperature and light. All are generated from definitions. | Owner | Crafting, construction, jobs and devices had mechanisms and no way for a player to reach them. The devices specification referred to a settings screen that did not exist. | 12 §6.2, §10.7; 16 §4.2; package G18. |
| D-209 | A site can be founded in play, given roles, held and dissolved, and is then a site like any other. A closed space can be made into an area by `enclose`, which keeps itself up to date and is marked breached when it no longer closes. | Owner | A base built by hand had no job board, stock, holder or identity, and could not be the target of anything. A hut was not a room until a map said so. | 09 §13.5; 02 §8.3; 10 §11.1, invariant X7; packages L5, W7, WG9, G8. |
| D-210 | A device may have a structure: a map of what must stand around it. It works while the cells match, and the cells are then the machine, with ports and holds at marked cells. | Owner | Machines larger than a cell, and machines that work only when built in the right shape, are the heart of the building mods this engine takes after. | 16 §4.5, invariant G14; package G16. |
| D-211 | Rates, capacities and speeds of devices may be reactive expressions of the device and its place. | Owner | Sun, wind, current and heat as sources of power were implied and not stated. | 16 §5.2; package G3. |
| D-212 | Items may age: a straight line from 0 to 1 at a rate that reads where the item is, with stages and something it turns into. Containers may state what they keep for their contents. Time spent unloaded uses the rate found at loading. | Owner | Spoiling, rusting, maturing and expiring had no mechanism, so cold storage had no purpose. The straight line keeps a larder of thousands free of polling. | 06 §9.4; packages E8, G2. |
| D-213 | Know-how is a set of named things held by a mind, required by recipes and suppliable instead by a manual or a loaded pattern at the station. It is gained by upbringing, teaching, reading and research. There is no ladder of technology in the engine. | Owner | "Knowledge" was required by recipes and kept nowhere. Patterns that machines need give blueprints a physical form. | 16 §8.5; 12 §10.7; package G17. |
| D-214 | Track may run vertically. A lift is a small vessel on vertical track with a hoist, sent by a signal. | Owner | The world model named an elevator as a moving piece of world and nothing said how one worked. | 11 §5.2; package V6b. |
| D-215 | Characters have a posture, defined by content, that scales height and with it bands, cover and reach, and may change speed, movement, signature and available channels. Changing it is an action and effects may force it. | Owner | Shooting from behind low cover, lying down, being knocked down and getting up had no representation. | 06 §11.1; 07 §4.3; packages E10, C3. |
| D-216 | A projectile may have thrust and guidance. A seeker follows the strongest source of a signal it can sense; a launcher-guided shot follows what its launcher believes while the launcher keeps guiding; others steer for a fixed point or return. It remains a body. | Owner | Rockets, homing shots and things that come back had no mechanism. Guiding by what can be sensed keeps decoys, cover and belief meaningful. | 07 §11.1; 02 §15; packages C9, W14. |
| D-217 | Harm that is not a blow reaches a body as an addition to a pool by a route: breath, skin or any, from a field; or through matter, from a signal that exposes, with shielding by material. Worn items may guard a route, and air of one's own guards breath. | Owner | Gas, sickness and radiation were only energy on the skin, so masks, filters, sealed suits and lead walls did nothing unless a mod wrote all of it. | 02 §16; 15 §3.1; 06 §9.1; packages W16, S2, E2, E8. |
| D-218 | Elements may carry a contact surface that strikes what walks into them, lands on them or is thrown against them, with the energy of the arrival. | Owner | Spikes, wire and broken glass should hurt by the same rule as a blade, and a fall onto them should differ from a fall onto stone. | 02 §5.4; 07 §6.5; packages C2, W3. |
| D-219 | A creature in flight can be struck, and the push adds to its velocity. One who leapt keeps their channels and may run actions that need no footing. `closing_energy` gives the energy of a mass at the closing speed, for attacks to add. | Owner | Striking someone before they land, attacking from a leap and the weight of a charge were all unstated. | 07 §3.5, §7.1; 03 §6; packages C2, C5, T2. |
| D-220 | Ammunition is items. A round names its projectile and energy; a magazine is a hold that gives rounds up in order. Weapons that draw on a store still use a cost. | Owner | Rounds have to be found, made, carried and mixed. A store of a resource could do none of that. | 07 §11.5; 06 §9.1; package C10. |
| D-221 | A body whose flight leaves loaded space is finished by arithmetic over recorded ground heights, and lands in full if its landing place is loaded or as a pending edit if not. Unloaded buildings on the way are not struck. | Owner | Long shots and long throws should not load everything beneath them. | 02 §15.4; packages W14, W17. |
| D-222 | Text shown to a player is written for a reader. `name(x)` gives what that reader calls someone: "you", a known name, or a description of what they made out. Helpers supply articles, pronouns, verb agreement, plurals and lists from a `language` definition. Personas carry pronouns. Reading another's true name in ordinary text is a lint warning. | Owner | A log line that prints a disguised character's real name breaks rule R11 in the one place the player reads most. English output also needed "you" and basic grammar. | 05 §4.6; 15 §8.1; 12 invariant V9; packages K9, X4, S6. |
| D-223 | A substance is one definition across its forms: loose field, vapour, piped resource and still material. Containers hold amounts and mixtures. A dose reaches a body by ingest, inject, skin or breath and adds to pools. Striking surfaces can be coated. Pools may name a substance that spills when they leak. | Owner | Poison, drink, medicine, venom and fuel were each wired by hand in every form. Nothing could be poured, mixed, swallowed or put on a blade. | 16 §9.5, invariant G15; 06 §6.1, §9.1; package G19. |
| D-224 | Traits, modes, postures and worn things may carry a look: glyph changes, a repeating animation, an aura and a label, shown for as long as they last to viewers who can perceive them. | Owner | Effects were one-shot. Burning, guarding, charging and transformed states looked like nothing. | 12 §4.4; 06 §5, §11.1; packages E3, E10, U4, U5. |
| D-225 | Colours are definitions with a word and a sixteen-colour fallback. Items and creatures may carry a colour of their own, which glyphs draw and descriptions name. Colour must not be the only thing that tells two things apart, and the linter checks it. | Owner | A witness's "figure in red" must be red on screen. Terminals and players who cannot tell colours apart must lose nothing essential. | 12 §4.1, §4.3, invariant V10; 06 §9.1; packages E8, U1, U2, U4. |
| D-226 | Ambience is a presentation-only definition, named by weather states, media, skies and area kinds: a tint, a dimming and particles. | Owner | Rain, dusk, fog and being under water had no look. What they do to perception is already simulated and stays there. | 12 §6.4; 09 §14.1; package U4. |
| D-227 | Names of people, places and vessels come from `names` definitions, drawn by positional seed. | Owner | Generated worlds had no way to name anything. | 10 §11.5; package WG9. |
| D-228 | Text may be styled inline with tags that name a theme style or a colour. | Owner | The text widget promised inline styles and gave no syntax. | 12 §4.3; 05 §4.6; package U1. |
| D-229 | A thing that fills several cells may give a shape: characters for each cell of its footprint, by facing. | Owner | Large creatures and small vehicles drew one glyph repeated. | 12 §4.1; package U4. |
| D-230 | Coloured light is offered as an experimental feature: a light may name a colour and the screen tints by what reaches each cell. Perception is unchanged and the simulation never reads it. Mods must opt in. | Owner | Wanted because it could be interesting, without committing to its cost or to colour affecting what characters see. | 15 §3.3 with its unresolved questions; 04 §8.4; 12 §6.1; package S10. |
| D-231 | An action parameter may be a list of another type, and a list of cells is a route. Parameters may carry a `check` that pickers show as advice. A route picker draws the path step by step. | Owner | Movement planned in advance and then carried out exactly could not be given to an action. Found by testing the design against a power that divides a second into frames. | 03 §6.3; 12 §9.3; 08 §9.5; packages T2, U7. |
| D-232 | A space imposed on the world is documented as a pattern, with no new mechanism: a bound, journalled area with a map applied over it, rules on the area kind that reach everyone inside, a contest when two meet, a rewind of the cells when it ends, and suppression afterwards. A realm is used instead when the inside differs in size or law. | Owner | Every piece existed and nothing showed them together. Each mod would have rebuilt it differently. | 08 §8.7, §9.6; package A10. |
| D-233 | A binding may be named, and a cost may be drawn from the thing so bound in place of the ability's owner. | Owner | A companion that holds its master's reserve could not pay for what the master did. | 08 §5.2, §7.3; packages A3, A7. |
| D-234 | The mind context is defined and enforced. Every registry entry says whether it is safe for belief. Whatever is reached from a goal, method, tactic, action default or ordinary screen compiles in it. A mind may read the shape of the world, not its state; what it has noticed is kept as noted things. An action's `requires` is tested against truth only when it starts, and `requires_at_start` is never shown beforehand. Previews run hook handlers dry. | Design, from the pre-build audit | Six independent readers each found ways a character or a player could learn what they had not perceived. Rule R11 was stated and not yet closed. | 05 §7.3; 09 §3.4; 15 §6, §7.1, §7.3; 03 §6.1; 07 §10, §13; 02 §23; 12 §10.4; 16 §8.3; 11 §15. |
| D-235 | Hooks exist before every operation a mod would plausibly refuse or alter, each with a line saying what it precedes and what a handler may change. Mods can define hooks of their own and learn the outcome. A cancellation carries a reason, and mods define reasons. | Design, from the pre-build audit | Immunities, taxes, locks and refusals were promised as rules on hooks that did not exist. A second mod could not reach into a first. | Hook tables in 03, 06, 07, 08, 11, 13, 16; 05 §8.1, §10.5; 14 §15. |
| D-236 | Engine limits and thresholds are settings in reserved namespaces, listed in each document and set by profiles and scenarios. The engine names no content id: the kinetic damage type, the devastation a damage type leaves, the damage pressure does and the signal that `noise` emits are nominated by data, with a load error when absent. | Design, from the pre-build audit | Constants were unnamed or named three ways with no way to change them, and four places broke the rule that no definition id appears in engine code. | 04 §10; Settings lines throughout; 02 §23; 15 §3.2. |
| D-237 | Renamed to end collisions: `attach_rule` and `detach_rule`; `make_notable` and `make_anonymous`; `advance_track`; `occupies` on postures; `set_motion_mode`; `knows_how`; `fitted`; `network_members`; `lane_drive`; `outlet` for a place knowledge is published; `base` for what a map's ground level sits on. | Design, from the pre-build audit | One name meant two things in eleven places, which a single registry cannot hold. | 02, 03, 05, 06, 08, 09, 10, 11, 13, 16. |
| D-238 | Being carried by a moving floor, a listing deck or a current is a relocation at a named step, not a push. | Design, from the pre-build audit | As written, a walkway would have staggered its riders and never moved them: the push rule has no outcome between nothing and a stagger. | 16 §9.4; 11 §4.7, §10.6. |
| D-239 | The interface toolkit is specified to the level of fields: each widget's fields and events, what `app` exposes, the screen for each role chosen by the profile, preferences and overlays as definitions, and a rule's way to ask for a screen. Bindings show the moment playback has reached. Cutscenes play only for what the party perceived. | Design, from the pre-build audit | A replacement main menu could not have been written from name lists, and two paths showed the player things before or without their character perceiving them. | 12 §6.2, §8.1, §10.3 to §10.7, §13; 04 §3.3. |
| D-240 | Group knowledge counts as told to every member when it is added. An order is kept on whoever received it and acted on by a goal. Enemies and friends are read from relation kinds flagged `opposes` and `sides_with`. Groups can be made and dissolved in play. | Design, from the pre-build audit | The first was happening silently and is now a stated simplification. Orders were described three ways. The AI's idea of an enemy had no source. | 15 §9.1, §9.2, §10; 09 §3.4. |
| D-241 | The definition of done requires every registered verb, event, hook, property, query and setting to state its arguments, subjects and fields, and to appear in the generated reference; where a specification only names one, the package that builds it completes the specification. | Design, from the pre-build audit | About three hundred identifiers are named in the specifications and fewer than a third have their arguments written out. Writing all of them before any is built would be guesswork; requiring it as each is built, with a test, is not. | CLAUDE.md; 14 §9.2; plans, review additions. |
| D-242 | Field rules, release shapes, exposure routes, engine flags, ground steps, transform steps, crossings and street patterns are fixed native sets, recorded as known limits of the modding surface. | Design, from the pre-build audit | They cannot be added to from data and the specifications did not say so. | 02 §23; 10 §13. |
| D-243 | Building starts on a beta path: a playable, moddable terminal game covering the world, destruction, people, combat, powers, devices, crafting, saving and modding, with vehicles, the cold simulation, incidents, the director and cases left for after. The beta plan's wave order replaces the other plans' wave order; package texts still apply to what is built. A package whose beta subset is built is marked `beta`, with what is stubbed. | Owner | The owner asked for a real beta, comprehensive and fun without glaring holes, and left the judgement of scope to the design. Following 211 packages in dependency order would reach something playable last. | docs/plans/beta.md; CLAUDE.md. |
| D-244 | `StateHash` is the trait that state types implement; the value it produces is a `Hash128`. The hasher is MurmurHash3 (`x64_128`, seed 0) over a stated byte encoding: integers little-endian at their own width, a length before anything of variable length, a `Real` by its exact bits. | Design, from building the foundation | Document 01 used one name for the trait and for the value. A public algorithm with published vectors can be checked by a tool that shares no code with the engine. | 01 §4, §5.3. The reference vectors in `clash-core` are the contract; changing the encoding of a type changes every golden hash. |
| D-245 | The generator is xoshiro256\*\* seeded through SplitMix64. The positional hash is five rounds of the SplitMix64 mixer over seed, domain, column, storey and salt. A domain or stream name becomes a number by 64-bit FNV-1a. A stream's seed is a function of the simulation's seed and the stream's name alone. | Design, from building the foundation | Published algorithms have known answers to test against. A stream that depended on which other streams exist, or on the order of first use, would make one system's randomness depend on another's. | 01 §4. Changing any of these changes every generated world and every golden hash. |
| D-246 | `Real` never holds NaN and may hold an infinity. Where IEEE arithmetic would give NaN, the plain operators and functions give zero; `Real::new` and the `try_` functions return an error instead. The two zeros compare equal but hash as different states. | Design, from building the foundation | Document 01 asked for "an error or a defined fallback" without choosing. Simulation code wants total functions; the rule language wants to be told (05 §4.5). Both are served. | 01 §4. A division by zero in physics code gives an infinity or zero and never a crash. |
| D-247 | A `LookupMap` cannot be iterated, but it hands out its contents sorted by key and serialises and hashes in that order. | Design, from building the foundation | State held in one must still be saved and hashed. An order sorted by key does not depend on history or hashing, which was the whole objection to iterating a hash map. | 01 §4. Each sorted read allocates and sorts; what is walked often belongs in a `BTreeMap`. |
| D-248 | `serde_json` is always built with `float_roundtrip`. | Design, from building the foundation | Without it a float may read back one unit in the last place from what was written (seen with `-1.5e33`), so a `Real` that passed through JSON would break R1 and R7. | The workspace's dependency table turns it on for every crate. |
| D-249 | The timeline refuses what it cannot do with a `TimeError` instead of panicking. `pop` leaves the time alone when nothing is due by its limit, and `advance_to` moves it. A wake-up's remaining local time is kept exactly and only its due tick is rounded, up. Destroying a clock moves its child clocks to its parent. Processes live in a `ProcessTable` with `take` and `settle` for an owner that holds its own table, and `remove` to force-resolve. | Design, from building the foundation | Rules can name a clock that is gone or ask for a negative rate (R6). An advance with no limit would otherwise run an empty queue to the end of time. Rounding local time down at every change loses time for good; rounding only the due tick does not accumulate. | 03 §3.1, §3.3, §3.6. A wake-up fires within one tick of the exact moment however many times its clocks change. |
| D-250 | The determinism lints live in the workspace's `clippy.toml`. A crate outside the simulation opts out with a `clippy.toml` of its own; inside it only a wrapper that makes a disallowed item safe may allow it, with a reason. The toolchain is pinned to 1.97.0. The check that size literals appear only in `clash_world::dims` joins `cargo xtask check` with W1, which creates that module. | Design, from building the foundation | A per-crate file survives a crate's source being rewritten, where an attribute in `lib.rs` might not. There is nothing for the literal check to check until the module exists. | 01 §4; plans/world-model.md F1. |
| D-251 | State is written to disk in the engine's own binary encoding, `clash_core::codec`: a serde format that does not describe itself. Unsigned integers and lengths are LEB128, signed integers zig-zag LEB128, `bool`, `u8` and `i8` one byte, floats their IEEE bits little-endian, text and sequences a length and then their contents, structs their fields in order, enums a variant index. Whatever is stored is a blob: a 32-byte header (the magic `C2DB`, a four-byte kind, a version, the body's length, a 128-bit checksum by the state hasher over kind, version and body), then the body as one LZ4 block. Decoding checks every length against the bytes that remain, requires every item of a sequence or map to take at least one byte, limits nesting to 128 levels and refuses leftover bytes. What could not be read back safely is refused when writing: sequences of unknown length, items that take no bytes, skipped fields, deeper nesting. | Design, from building the beta | The documents named no encoding, and the dependency list allows no crate for one. A format the engine owns is a contract that tests can pin byte for byte. A damaged save must be an error and never a crash, an unbounded allocation or a hang (R6). | 01 §11.1; 02 §11. `lz4_flex` joins `clash-core` with its default features, under which its block codec contains no `unsafe`; they bring `twox-hash` with them, which nothing calls. State types cannot use `flatten`, untagged or internally tagged enums, or `skip_serializing_if`. Changing the layout of a stored type means a new version of the blobs that hold it. |
| D-252 | Noise for generation is lattice noise built on the positional hash and `Real` alone, in `clash_gen::noise`: value noise, gradient noise (sixteen unit slopes in the plane and the 26 directions to neighbouring lattice points in space, scaled to reach exactly ±1), fractal sums with ridged and billowed folds, exact cellular noise and domain warping, each keyed by a seed and a domain, with a salt of its own for every use and every octave. A noise field's `scale` is in tracts, `lacunarity` and `gain` are optional, `range` defaults to 0 to 1, and its `shape` is `flat`, `island`, `continent` or `ridged` by stated formulas. | Design, from building the beta | Document 10 named octaves, scale, the shapes and the range and gave no formula for any, and two builds of one definition must give the same world (X4). Tracts are the unit every other size in document 10 is given in. | 10 §9.1, §13. The reference vectors in `clash-gen`, checked against an independent implementation, are the contract: changing a formula changes every generated world. An `island` or `continent` needs the extent of its realm from whoever binds the field. |
| D-253 | An arrow in the workspace layout carries what its target depends on: a crate may depend directly on any crate its arrows already reach. | Design, from building the beta | Rust needs a direct dependency to name a crate. `clash-gen` uses `Real` and the positional hash, and its arrow to `clash-world` already implies `clash-core`. | 01 §3. No new edge between layers is allowed by this: only ones that already exist through another crate. |
| D-290 | A colour in a frame is its exact `rgb` together with its sixteen-colour fallback, in cells and in sprites alike. A golden frame is text: the grid of characters, each cell's colours as indices into a palette listed with it, attributes, sprites and effects; `BLESS=1` rewrites it. | Design, from building presentation | 12 §4.2 gave a sprite a bare `Rgb`, which left a terminal nothing to fall back to and made every frontend look colours up. Listing the palette keeps the file readable and shows a changed colour as one changed line. | 12 §4.2, §12. Frontends map colours with no lookup. A frame cell is 16 bytes. |
| D-291 | A layout node is an object with exactly one key naming its kind, whose value holds that kind's own fields; size, `frame` and the common fields sit beside it, and a widget may carry the common fields inside its own object instead. Rows are named `rows` everywhere, a `figure` widget names a `figure` definition and a `shade`, and `min_size` is `[columns, rows]`. Rows, options, sizes, the `figure` definition and each widget's remaining fields are written out in 12 §10.2 and §10.3. | Design, from building presentation | The example screen of 12 §10.1 used `of` where the field table said `rows`, and gave `figure` a field the table does not have; the table governs.
