Entering only what is missing lets one function serve the loader that was given the registry, the one that was not, and a simulation over definitions loaded without it. The expression registry has no notion of an optional subject. | 05 §5.3, §8.1; 04 §7.3. A package adds a table and a line in `engine_properties`. Getters take `&Sim` and read truth; setters take `&mut Sim` and call the engine's own functions. `clash-data`'s tests of expressions and `clash-gen`'s keep registries of their own and are unaffected. |
| D-466 | Nothing the simulation registers is safe for belief unless it follows from a reference alone: a subject's `kind`; a cell's coordinates, grid, tract and realm; a tract's coordinates, realm and corner; an element's layer and cell; universal time; and `dist`, `dir`, `offset`, `now`, `setting` and `has_def`. Everything about an entity and everything that reads a cell's contents is truth, the shape of the world that 02 §23 allows a mind included, and so are `los` and `light`. | Design, from building properties | 02 §23 lets a mind read the shape of the world but withholds whatever carries the tag `hidden` until it is perceived, and nothing can tell what a character has perceived until document 15 is built. A property marked safe too early tells every mind what it has not seen (01 R11); one marked unsafe only refuses an expression at load, and can be relaxed later without breaking any mod that loads today. | 05 §5.4, §7.3; 02 §23. A mind context reads these of its own subject and of nothing else. The marks are revisited when perception supplies the belief side; 05 §7.3's sentence that `los` is safe between places a character knows describes that state. |
| D-467 | A variable is kept where its scope lives: an entity's in the kernel entity, a cell's in its chunk, an area's, a site's, a tract's and a realm's in their records, a global one in the cosmos. `Sim::set_var` is the one way any of them changes; a cell's goes through `WorldEdit`. The world gained `Cosmos::set_global_var`, `set_realm_var`, `set_tract_var`, `set_region_var` and `tract_var`. Reading gives what was set, else the declared default for a subject of the declared kind, else null, and null of a subject that is gone. A declared variable is held to its kind of subject and its type. A watched variable raises `var_changed` when what it reads as changes, with the old and new readings, and binds what it lives on under the name of its kind. | Design, from building variable storage | 02 §9.1 and §8 already give every record a variable map, saved and hashed with it, and gave three of them no way to be written. A store of the simulation's own would duplicate them. 05 §17 gives `var_changed` a subject without a kind, and an event's subjects are typed: one name for each kind lets a rule that listens read `self.var.kills` or `site.var.alarm_raised` and be checked. Comparing readings and not stored values makes unsetting a variable back to its default a change, and setting it to its default none. | 05 §6, §17; 02 §23. Additive functions in `clash-world` (`vars.rs`, and one in `cosmos.rs`). `region`, `subgrid`, `action`, `scenario` and `legacy` scopes wait for their kinds. The reasons are `subject.gone`, `vars.wrong_scope`, `vars.wrong_type` and `vars.too_deep`. With `var_changed` among the events a save may hold, the save layout is version 3. |
| D-468 | The host is `SimHost`, over `&Sim`, with what the caller bound by slot. `Sim::host` only reads; `Sim::with_random` lends the host a copy of the rule stream and writes it back when the evaluations are done. Warnings are kept on the host, sixty-four at most. `Sim::assign` writes a compiled place: it evaluates the subject and the keys reading only, then sets a variable through `set_var` or a property through its registered setter. With keys the value is read, changed and written back whole; a key into nothing makes a map; an entry set to null is removed; `add` adds to numbers and appends to lists, starting from a variable's default. A local and an event field are refused: whoever runs the rule keeps them. The reader's text helpers answer in plain forms. | Design, from building the host | The expression language's host reads and never writes (05 E1), and a getter that could borrow the simulation mutably could not be handed a view of it. Random draws are the one thing an evaluation changes, and a stream is small: copying it out and back keeps every getter a plain reader. 05 §10.3 names places and leaves the write to the simulation; the rule runtime, built beside this, needs it as a function. | 05 §5.3, §10.3, §14. `Bindings::bind_event` gives what an event binds, slot by slot. The reasons are `write.not_settable`, `write.bad_value`, `write.bad_key`, `write.cannot_add` and `write.not_a_subject`. Keys are evaluated without random draws. |
| D-469 | Whoever loads whole mods gives the loader the engine's registry: `clash_scene::engine_loader(types)` is `Loader::new(types)` with `clash_sim::engine_registry()`, and `clash-tools` loads through it. `mods/core` holds a variable and three functions under `data/rules/` that read real properties. A crate below the simulation that loads a bundled mod does not register the `function` and `variable` types, and passes them over with the other types it does not know. `loc.in_area` takes an area kind and `loc.in_area_tagged` a tag; `dir` gives the signs of the offset; `tract.devastation` is the stored 0 to 255. | Design, from joining the registry to the tools | A linter that checked expressions against the built-ins alone would pass a mod the game refuses. Bundled functions name kinds of subject, which only the simulation registers, so a crate below it cannot compile them and has no use for them (D-450). 05 named `in_area('vault')` and `dir(a, b)` without saying what the argument or the result is. | 04 §7.3, §13; 05 §5.2, §17; 02 §23. `clash-gen`'s test of the bundled maps and its `map_ascii` example no longer register `function` and `variable`. A map that came to call a function of a mod would need the simulation's registry to be checked. |
| D-530 | A `control` has `name`, `doc` and `do`, one UI effect or a list. A key's name is offered to the focused widget, the menu hotkeys and the screen's `keys`, then to the controls, then to the toolkit. A bare name in a keymap means the control of that name in the keymap's namespace, or else the first by qualified id. A control's effects are compiled as a `ui_part`'s are, with `it` bound to the place picked. Keymap contexts are free names; the engine knows `menu` and `debug`, and the `ui` mod adds `play` and `targeting`. | Design, from building input | 12 §9.2 gave an example and no fields, called the play context `map`, and wrote a control's parameter as `"west"`, which section 10.5 reads as an expression naming nothing. Putting controls after a screen's `keys` lets one key walk in play and move a cursor in a picker without a second set of names. | 12 §9.2, §10.3. `ControlDef`, `UiDefs::control`, `Screen::input_with`, `UiEvent::Control`. The example is corrected: the context is `play` and the text is `'west'`. |
| D-531 | `rebind` changes the keymap in force and nothing else; an empty name takes a key's meaning away; the application is then told and gives stored bindings back through `Scene::rebind`. A control names its action as `open` names a screen, and an action no mod defines is not a load error: the command goes to the simulation as written and is refused there. The `ui` mod names `step` and `go_to` bare. | Design, from building input | The `ui` mod must not require a content mod, and an engine that named the walking action would break rule R2. 04 §4.3 makes a dangling `Ref` an error, but an effect's screen is already resolved when it runs (D-433), and a bare name that means whichever mod defines it is how a total conversion supplies its own walking. | 12 §9.2. The linter does not report a control whose action is missing; it is listed as a gap. |
| D-532 | `act` with `"needed"` or `"all"` is carried out by the scene: it reads the action's roles and parameters from its definition, works each default out in the actor's mind through the host with the values before it as locals, asks with one picker for each parameter to be asked for, and submits one whole command with the action's qualified id. A default that comes to null in the scene is left for the simulation. Closing a picker abandons the command. The effects after the `act` run once the command is accepted. Where the scene has no definition of the action, the command is handed over as written with `collect` saying what was wanted. | Design, from building input | D-433 left the command marked for the kernel to ask. The kernel never waits (R5), so the asking is the scene's, and a command that is whole when it is submitted is what the input log can replay. A default may draw at random, and the simulation's streams are not the scene's. | 12 §9.3, §10.5; 03 §6.3. `clash_scene::control::ask`. `clash-scene` reads `clash_sim::action::ActionDef`; nothing in `clash-sim` changed. Reaction prompts use the same path for a reaction's parameters. |
| D-533 | The picker for a parameter is said by the `act` effect's `pick`, or else by its type: `entity`, `cell`, `route` for a list of cells, a kind's own name, `number`, `text`, `multi` for other lists and `list` for the rest; `from` on a number or a text makes it `list`. A direction has no type of its own (05 §3), so an effect asks for one by `pick`. Candidates come from the effect's `from`, or the host, or for an entity the view model. Every picker is offered `action`, `param`, `title`, `value`, `options`, `min` and `max` and takes those it declares. An answer the parameter cannot hold is refused and the picker stays; for a list each answer is an entry and the new `picker` effect ends the list, takes an entry back or toggles one. | Design, from building input | 12 §9.3 listed pickers by what is picked, and the expression language has no direction, item or choice type to hang them on. Choosing by type with an override in the effect keeps actions free of presentation, and `clash-sim` untouched. | 12 §9.3, §10.5, §10.7. `PickerEffect`, `ParamAsk`, `SceneHost::candidates`. The route picker's numbered path and several characters at once are not built; U7 is `beta`. |
| D-534 | Controls reach the camera through a new effect, `camera`: `move`, `storey`, `zoom`, `cycle`, `release` and `turn`. A screen that declares `cursor_sight`, `cursor_distance`, `cursor_target`, `cursor_reach`, `cursor_why`, `cursor_advice`, `cursor_index`, `cursor_count` or `picker_entries` has them kept up to date. `cursor_reach` previews the action's `requires` and `cursor_advice` its parameter's `check`, both in the actor's mind. While a map picker is open the line to the cursor is drawn with the theme mark `line`, over open ground only. | Design, from building input | A picker is a screen, and a screen had no way to move a cursor or to learn what lies under it. Variables are what a screen can already bind and test. Plain names such as `count` collided with a screen's own variables in an existing test, hence the prefix. 03 §6.1 compiles `requires` in the mind context so that it can be previewed, which is what reach is. | 12 §6.3, §6.5, §10.5. `CameraEffect`, `clash_scene::picker`, `WorldView::show_line`, `ViewMarks::line`. The view model, the camera and mind-context expressions are all that is read. |
| D-535 | A click on a map view picks the cell, or, where a free cursor is on show elsewhere, first moves the cursor. Picking runs the view's `on_pick`, or the control `click`; the second button runs `click_second`; `accept` in a focused map view with an `on_pick` picks under the cursor. `it` is a map with `cell`, `entity`, `x`, `y` and `z`. The wheel over a map view zooms. | Design, from building input | 12 §9.4 named the `click` control and §10.3 said `it` is the place picked, without saying what a place is or how a cursor and a click agree. Moving first is what a list does with its selection. | 12 §9.4. `UiEvent::ViewPick`, `WorldView::map_cell_at`. The inspection tooltip on the map waits for look and inspect (U9). |
| D-536 | The scene learns of a decision from `SceneHost::pending_decision`, a `PendingDecision`: who decides and, for a reaction, the window, its options and the time left, or for a choice the prompt and options. The roles are `decision.free` (the play screen), `decision.reaction` and `decision.choice`, replacing `reaction` and `choice`. A prompt answers with an option's number; a reaction prompt's null passes. The answers are the commands `react`, `pass` and `choose`, by name. | Design, from building input | The kernel's `DecisionKind` has only `Free` yet, and the scene cannot name the kernel's command type (D-432). Grouping the three roles under one prefix matches `picker.`, and makes the free decision a role a profile can point at its own play screen. | 12 §9.5, §10.7. `clash_scene::control::decide`, `Scene::sync_decision`. The simulation implements `pending_decision` and takes the three commands. |
| D-537 | The scene's seam to the simulation gains five reads beside `pending_decision`: `cell_value` and `cell_of` between a cell and the simulation's value for it, `sprite_value` and `sprite_of` between a sprite's number and the perceived thing, and `candidates`. Names shown by pickers come from the built-in `name`, which the host answers. The profile's `screens` in `profiles/default.json` name the `ui` mod's screens for every role it has one for. | Design, from building input | A handle's payload means something only to the simulation (05 §3), and a sprite is known by a number its feeder chooses (D-350), so the scene cannot make either value itself. | 12 §3, §9.3. All five default to nothing, so a host without them compiles. `clash_scene::testing::FakeHost` implements them and gained a `loc` kind, `dist`, `at` and `name`. |
| D-470 | The rule runtime is written against an interface, the rule host, and not against the simulation: reads through the expression host, and beside them setting a variable or a property, carrying out a verb by id, raising an event, the local time of an owner, the rules a subject's definitions carry, whether a subject is simulated, and where reports go. The rules of a set of definitions are a `RuleSet`, derived and not saved; what is kept between runs is `RuleState`, plain state. One function, `RuleSet::deliver`, finds and runs everything that answers an event, global and attached, so the kernel registers one handler for each event type and none for each rule or attachment. | Design, from building the rule runtime | The subjects and properties of the simulation were being built at the same time and could not be seen. The console was built this way and bound later without change. A handler for each attachment would have made attachments something to register again after every load; as state they need nothing. | 05 §13.1; package K5. `clash_sim::rule`: `RuleHost`, `RuleSet`, `RuleState`, `Occurrence`, `testing::FakeHost`. Binding to `Sim` is the six steps of the module's documentation. Rules run at one place among the engine's own handlers of an event. |
| D-471 | A list of effects is kept by its field as the loader's own tree and compiled in `Def::compile`, into a tree of nodes the interpreter walks with an explicit stack. Each argument is read from the tree as an expression field and compiled in the scope the effect stands in. An effect written with several keys is told by the key that names it; any other has one key. A verb with a direct argument takes it as any value that is not an object, or as an object none of whose keys is an argument. A definition type that holds effects compiles them with `cx.compile` and checks their calls with `Effects::validate`. Mistakes have codes `E0681` to `E0684`. | Design, from building the rule runtime | A verb's arguments cannot be read until the registry is known, which is after typed loading, and a typed read cannot tell `then` from a verb's argument before it has seen every key. Reading arguments as expression fields keeps one meaning for a literal, a quantity, an id and a value source, in a rule and anywhere else. What a rule a call names needs can only be seen once every rule is compiled. | 04 §7.3, §8.2; 05 §10.1, §14. `clash_data::from_node_at` reads a piece of a tree with its whole JSON path; group `068` of the compile stage's codes. Lists of effects nest at most 32 deep. `action::EffectsSource` is replaced by `rule::Effects`. |
| D-472 | A rule binds the subjects of its event, then `self` if it is attached, then the subjects it declares in `subjects`, then `global`. A rule at the top level of a file is global unless it says `"attached": true`; one written in place is attached. A rule that is called takes each subject it binds from the subject its caller binds under the same name, and says which it expects in `subjects`; calling it where one is not bound is a load error. It has locals of its own and cannot name its caller's. | Design, from building the rule runtime | 05 said a called rule sees its caller's subjects, which cannot be checked at load, and invariant E3 asks that every name be. It did not say how a rule at the top level is told from one meant to be attached with `attach_rule`, nor what kind `self` is. `global` was listed among the names of an expression and bound nowhere. | 05 §9.1, §9.2, §9.3, §9.5. `EocDef::subjects`, `SubjectSource`. The kind of an attached rule's `self` is that of the event's subject `as` names; a recurring attached rule declares it. |
| D-473 | A rule with at least one parameter is entered in the expression registry as a verb under its qualified id, by a step that runs before anything is compiled, so that it can be written as an effect and its arguments checked where it is called. The effects of the rule system come first, then the engine's verbs, then rules. A rule has `returns`, the type `return` must give. `run` and `emit` as values are written as the value of a `let` and nowhere else, and what `run` gives is typed `any`. | Design, from building the rule runtime | While one definition is compiled the others cannot be seen; the registry can. A call that returns in the middle of an expression would need the interpreter to stop inside the expression machine; as an effect of its own it is one more frame. The registry has no place for the type a verb gives back. | 05 §9.5, §10.3, §10.5. `rule::register_defs` adds the step. A rule written in place, or with no parameters, is not a verb; the second is run with `run`. The console's command table and the reference list rules with parameters beside the engine's verbs. |
| D-474 | `once` and `cooldown` count the runs in which the condition held. They are kept for each rule and owner, with no owner for a global rule and for a rule that is called, and they and rule groups govern a rule however it is run. Detaching a rule forgets what was kept for it. A group is on or off as `set_rule_group` last switched it, and otherwise as its `enabled` says; `"on": null` clears the switch. Until reactive expressions exist `enabled` is evaluated when a rule of the group is about to run. | Design, from building the rule runtime | 05 named the fields and did not say what a run is, whether a call counts, or what happens to a curse laid twice. A rule whose condition failed has done nothing a modder would call running. A group that stopped only triggered rules would leave an optional rule set half on. | 05 §9.1, §9.3, §9.6. `RuleState::record`, `set_group`. A group that is off costs one condition for each of its rules an event reaches, until package A1 keeps `enabled` current. |
| D-475 | A run has one step budget, shared with the rules it calls; each rule that answers an event and each queued rule is a run. A value is measured when an effect writes it, for size and for nesting: `rules.max_value_depth`, 32. At most `rules.max_queued` rules, 1,000, wait in the queue. What goes wrong while a rule runs is a fault with a code of the `rule.` group, the rule, and the definition and JSON path of the effect; the host thins faults by count as it does run-time warnings. Only the step limit ends a run; after any other fault the rule carries on. | Design, from building the rule runtime | A budget for each call would let thirty-two nested calls spend it thirty-two times over. The expression machine limits the size of a value and not its depth, and `"$x": "[$x]"` in a loop builds a value deep enough to exhaust the stack of whatever later compares, hashes or drops it. | 05 §13.2, §17. `RuleLimits`, `RuleFault`, `RULE_REASONS`, `FaultLimiter`. Hook nesting and the cascade limit stay the kernel's. |
| D-476 | `queue` calls a rule once the outermost rule in progress has finished: the rule, its arguments and its subjects are worked out when the effect runs, and queued rules run in the order they were queued, before anything else happens. The queue is not saved: it is empty at every safe point. | Design, from building the rule runtime | The package's brief named the effect and 05 did not have it. Timers, which would give "later", are a later package's; "after this rule" needs none, and is what a rule wants when it must not act in the middle of a hook. | 05 §10.5, §13.2. A rule queued by a queued rule runs after it. |
| D-477 | `set` takes a value of the place's type or null. `add` adds numbers, an int if both are, appends one element to a list, and counts something never set as nothing. `unset` removes a variable or an entry and makes a local null. Assigning to an entry of null makes a map. The keys of `switch` are texts, compared with the value as it prints, and are resolved at load where the value is an id. `for_each` goes through an expression that gives a list. `weighted` makes one draw. `emit` gives a `let` a map of the event's fields with `cancelled` and `reason`. `cancel_event` with no reason gives `events.cancelled`. | Design, from building the rule runtime | 05 §10.2 to §10.5 gave each effect a line and left its arguments, their types and its edge cases open, which the definition of done asks to be stated. | 05 §10.2, §10.3, §10.5. |
| D-478 | A `reason` definition has `text`, `values` and `doc`. `values` names what the reason carries, each with its type, and the sentence reads each by its name. The sentence is rendered when the reason is given. The reason's code is its qualified id, and it carries each value as a truth value, a number or a text; an id as its qualified id and anything else as it prints. | Design, from building the rule runtime | 14 §15 gave an example and no fields, and its sentence named a value the definition did not declare. The reason the whole simulation uses carries plain values only. | 14 §15; 05 §10.5. `rule::ReasonDef`. The engine's own reasons stay tables of constants. |
| D-479 | The events mods define are entered in the expression registry by a step of the rule module, each with its subjects, fields, and whether it is a hook; one already there is left alone, and one whose subjects name a kind the registry lacks is left out. Timer effects and a rule's `every` are read, checked and kept, their effects compiled, and report when they run. `for_each` over a query keeps the query as written and reports. | Design, from building the rule runtime | A rule cannot listen for a mod's event, nor raise one, unless the compiler knows its subjects and fields, and the kernel kept event types to itself until kinds of subject existed. Compiling what a timer will run finds its mistakes at load, before timers exist. | 05 §8.1, §10.4, §11. If the kernel enters `event` definitions itself, one of the two steps goes. Packages K6 and K7 fill in `Items::Query` and `EffectKind::Timer`. |
| D-520 | Bodies, hits and wounds are built ahead of the kernel in `clash_sim::body`, as stats were (D-449): functions that take the store of `Body`, the definitions, the store of `Physical`, containment and a `StatsCx`, and return what changed as values. `Body` is the fourth component type. The functions change pools through `stats` and never evaluate an expression: when a function's capacity moved, whoever called works the stats out again. `ComponentStore::owner_in_slot` is added. | Design, from building the beta | The package is built apart from the kernel and cannot raise an event or hold a host. An attribute that reads `self.fn.x` is evaluated against a host that reads the bodies, which cannot be borrowed while a body is being changed. | 06 §6.3, §6.4; packages E4, E5. `BodyCx`, `body::Change`. The kernel raises the events of 06 §7.6 from the changes, interrupts actions named by `SlotsLost`, passes `MassChanged` to the world and calls `stats::refresh` after `FunctionChanged`. The component store's tests that listed three component types now list four. |
| D-521 | The tissue extras are fields of the world's `material` type, which stores them and gives them no meaning: `leak { pool, rate, closes }`, `signal { pool, per_joule }` and `heal`, a share of a layer per second. `severs` is a field of `damage_type` in the same way. The pool is text, resolved by the body model in the material's own namespace and then every namespace in sorted order (D-283). `closes` is new: how long a breach of that tissue leaks before it closes by itself. | Design, from building the beta | 06 §6.1 and §13 put them on `material`, and the world cannot name a pool. Bleeding stops in minutes and tissue mends in weeks: one `heal` rate cannot give both, and a wound that never closed unaided would make every cut fatal. | 02 §5.4; 06 §6.1. A pool that does not exist is a load error at the material, without a line, and at every part built of it, with one. A body with no such pool does not leak. |
| D-522 | A part definition gives `mass`, `length` and `girth`; a part's area is its silhouette, `length × girth`, and every layer has it. Layers that leave `weight` out share what the others leave of 1, by thickness. `disable_below`, `leaves` and `inside` are fields of a part: `inside` lists organs that come with it, each named `<part>.<id>` in a body. A plan gives `height`, `footprint` and, for each part, an optional `scale`; it has exactly one root. A part of a body has a key, a number never reused in that body, and a name, the plan's `id`. | Design, from building the beta | 06 §6.1 used `disable_below` and girth without defining them, gave no area for the scale law or for exposure, and no height for `Physical`. `inside` was a row of the table with no form. Wounds and hits need a reference to a part that is `Copy` and survives other parts coming and going; rules need its name. | 06 §6.1, §6.2, §6.4. `PartKey`, `PartInst::name`. Plan mistakes are load errors at the entry. At most 1024 parts and 16 layers; organs nest at most eight deep. |
| D-523 | A function's capacity combines every part that provides it: one that works gives `provides × condition`, one that is disabled or gone gives nothing and still counts. Parts that are gone stay listed, with no layers and a state that says how. A part taken off by an edit is not listed and no longer counts. | Design, from building the beta | Read as "working parts only", `min` over two lungs rose to full when one was lost and `mean` over two eyes did not fall. What a body has lost has to be readable, and a template that removes a tail must not leave a body that is forever missing one. | 06 §6.3, §6.4. `PartState`, `Body::missing`, `Detach::Removed`. |
| D-524 | A layer's damage records how much of the part's width has been breached. The packet rule is applied to the volume under the contact, `toughness × min(area, part area) × thickness`, with the layer's damage; a breach then adds `cover × 255` to the layer and damage short of one `cover ×` what the rule gives, where `cover = min(1, span / girth)`, and never less than one 255th. A layer wholly gone is not in the way. | Design, from building the beta | 06 §7.2 said "record what the layer absorbed as damage" and not against what. Against the contact volume, a needle through an arm left its layers destroyed; against the whole layer, a blade through the muscle left the arm at full condition. What a limb can do depends on how much of its cross-section is cut, and `span` against `girth`, which already decides severing, measures that. | 06 §7.2; 07 §6.1. One cut across an arm disables it, a stab through it does not, and hits below a threshold never accumulate. A gone layer no longer stops a hit too weak to have affected it. |
| D-525 | A hit says where it arrives with `at`: a part, a height, anywhere, or spread; `part: Option` is gone. At most one part inside a breached part is struck, each with a chance of its area over the outer part's. Only a part `on` another is severed, by a hit that named or found that one part, and it comes off as it was before the hit. A part every layer of which is gone is destroyed; the root destroyed is the body in pieces, which is reported and decides nothing. Energy that a layer could not be affected by is returned, energy left over exits, and the report accounts for all that was delivered. | Design, from building the beta | Projectiles and contact surfaces arrive at a height or on no part in particular, which is not a spread hit. 06 §7.2 left open how many inner parts are struck, what a severed part looks like, and what happens to the root. Combat needs what was not absorbed for recoil (07 §6.3) and for carrying on (07 §6.2). | 06 §7.1, §7.2, §7.5. `HitAt`, `HitReport { struck, worn, absorbed, returned, exits, detached, destroyed }`, `Detached`. Worn layers are handed in as `WornLayer`s and what they took comes back as `WornHit`s for the item package. |
| D-526 | A wound's area is the tissue it lays open: the contact area or, where larger, the face of the cut, `min(span, girth) ×` the thickness gone through. Each layer it goes through has its share of that area by thickness and leaks `rate ×` its share. A breach where a wound of the same kind is open makes that wound worse. A part that comes off leaves a stump on its parent with the area of its cross-section. An open wound keeps its layers from healing; it closes after the longest `closes` of its leaking tissues; closed, its layers recover a 255th every `1 / (255 × heal)` seconds; it is healed, and removed, when they are whole. A body keeps one wake-up for the earliest of these, in the domain `Timer` with `arg` `1 << 62`, and keeps times as what is left. | Design, from building the beta | With the contact area of an edge, a deep cut leaked a hundredth of what a split lip did. Unmerged, a thousand cuts would be a thousand wounds and sources. 06 §7.3 said wounds "close and heal at their tissues' rates, as timers" and gave neither rule. Times kept as moments would need converting whenever the owner changed clock or left the simulation. | 06 §7.3. `Wound`, `body::on_wake`, `is_body_wake`, `suspend`, `resume`, `set_clock`. The kernel's own timers leave bit 62 of `arg` alone or ask `is_body_wake` first. `wound_reopened` is a new event. |
| D-527 | A spread hit gives every outer part a share of the energy by exposed area, `area × exposure`, over the whole part, and severs nothing. It destroys a body outright when it has full effect on every layer and carries at least `hit.overkill_factor` (default 10) times what breaching them all would take. An exposure report becomes a spread hit of `min(energy, intensity × volume)`; a body impact a spread hit of all the energy lost, or a hit anywhere with the contact surface struck; a field's damage rule an exposure of its level times what each level delivers. `dir` does not yet limit a spread hit. | Design, from building the beta | The world reports what was delivered to a cell of three cubic metres, of which a body takes its own volume's worth. "Many times over" needed a number and a guard: a great deal of energy too thinly spread to affect anything must do nothing. Nothing says which way a body faces. | 06 §7.4, §13 (`hit.overkill_factor`). `exposure_hit`, `impact_hit`, `field_hit`, `HitSettings`. |
| D-528 | Bodies are read through `entity.fn`, a subject of the kind `functions` with a property for each `body_function` as for stats (D-440), and through subjects of the kinds `part` and `wound`, whose handles hold the owner's slot, the low sixteen bits of its generation and the part's key or the wound's id. `parts` and `wounds` are list properties. None is settable and none is safe for belief. The fields of the events and of the hook `hit_incoming`, the properties and the reasons for refusal are written into 06 §6.7 and §7.6. `disable_part` and `restore_part` are new verbs. | Design, from building the beta | A handle has sixty-four bits, all of which an entity's id takes. Sixteen bits of generation tell a stale handle from a live one unless the slot was reused exactly 65,536 times while the handle was kept. 06 named the events and properties without their fields or types. What a body can do and how it is hurt is its owner's to know and anyone else's to perceive (01 R11). | 06 §6.7, §7.6, §13; 05 §5.1. `body::property`, `property_at`, `props::part_handle`. The queries of 05 §11 replace the lists when queries are built. |
| D-529 | Left out of bodies and hits for now: shared parts for unchanged bodies (D-185), the other edit verbs of 06 §6.5, bodies made of cells (06 §6.6), which parts face a hit, what a pool that names a substance spills, and benchmarks. The scripts `one_bad_cut` and `through_the_wall` are integration tests of the functions until the kernel holds bodies; the second covers only the turning of impact reports into hits. | Design, from building the beta | Each waits for a package that is not built: the kernel's wiring, items, body editing, world bodies, substances. A body must be able to be cut before any of them. | plans/first-playable.md E4, E5, both `beta`. |
| D-580 | A game is saved into one `redb` file per save, extension `.c2save`, in one transaction: the tract blobs of every tract that has been loaded and changed, the world's sections, the simulation's blob as the section `sim.state`, the list of tracts held as `save.tracts` and a metadata section `save.meta` (format version, name and summary, profile, mods with versions, fingerprint, seed, tick, experimental features, state hash, and bytes of the saver's own). `save_game`/`load_game` are the two functions an application calls; the same works against any store and as bytes. Loading gives the simulation a store in memory holding the save's tract blobs. | Design, from building K7 and TL13 | One file is one save, durable all at once or not at all; a load screen reads the metadata without loading; a loader can say what changed since. The kernel keeps the entities of unloaded tracts in its own state, so no entity sections are handed to the world. | 01 §11.2. `clash-save`: `save_game`, `load_game`, `SaveMeta`, `read_meta`, `verify`, `list_saves`. |
| D-581 | The console's host over the real simulation is `clash_save::host::GameHost`, in `clash-save`, which gains the arrow `clash-save → clash-scene`. It is not `SimHost`, which is the expression language's host. | Design, from building W11 and TL1 | The application and the tools both need it, and the application may not depend on the tools; `clash-save` is below both and already reaches the simulation. The host loads profiles with every definition type (the scene's `engine_types`) and draws frames through the scene's view model. | 01 §3; 14 §3.3. |
| D-582 | `clash-tools` depends on `clash-front-term` for the terminal world viewer. | Design, from building W11 | The viewer draws through the same terminal frontend as the game. | 01 §3; 14 §3.1. |
| D-583 | Test scripts run against the real simulation; exit code 3 of `scenario run` is gone. A comment before a script's first command may carry `# host: fake` (run against the fake host) or `# known failure: <why>` (expected to fail; the run fails if it passes). | Design, from registering the host | Supersedes the exit code 3 of D-345, which stood for "no host". The console's own script keeps testing the console; a bug in someone else's code is recorded as a failing script without failing every run. | 14 §7.2; `scenarios/README.md`. |
| D-584 | `gen <kind> size=N` makes a realm and loads N × N tracts from tract (0, 0), at most 16 a side, held by debug tickets of radius 0. `flat`, `city` and `country` name a generator, and the first realm definition in load order naming it is made; a realm definition's id is accepted too, and test scripts use one so that their hashes do not depend on which realms the mods define. The cursor goes to the middle of the block, on the highest cell that can be stood in. Each realm draws from the session's seed plus the number of realms made before it. | Design, from building W11 | The tools name no content: the kind of world names a generator, and data says which realm uses it. | 14 §3.3. |
| D-585 | The console's `damage`, `field` and `gen` change the world through `Sim::world` with the debug cause, followed by delivery of events and reconciling of tickets. They are not `Debug` commands and are not in the input log; the console's transcript replays them. | Design, from building the host | The kernel has no `Debug` command for them and is another package's to change. Test scripts, which replay from their lines, are not affected; `replay` between saves is, and says so. | 14 §3.3 and §15 (gaps). A `Debug` command for each is owed to the kernel. |
| D-586 | `damage` with no layer strikes the first element there is of the cell's block, fixture and floor, not each in turn; without `intensity` the packet has one pascal for each joule (its energy in one cubic metre), and without `kind` it is the kinetic damage type. | Design, from writing `wall_punch` | Striking every layer with the whole packet destroyed a wall and then its own rubble. | 14 §3.3. |
| D-587 | `clash-tools replay <from> <to>` replays the commands between two saves of one game and compares the end state with the later save by subsystem. The kernel's log has no hashes at intervals, so the first differing tick is not found. | Design, from building TL8 as far as cheap | It is what the kernel's input log makes possible now: a save carries its log since it was last taken, and the earlier save's log is a prefix of the later's. | 14 §8. Hashes at intervals in the log are owed to TL8. |
| D-588 | What the console keeps beside the simulation (its cursor, active character, selection, frame size, profile name and seed) is saved in the metadata's bytes of the saver's own and never hashed. | Design, from building the host | Loading must put the console back as it was for the save-and-load mode, and none of it is simulation state. | 01 §11.2. |
| D-490 | Bodies in flight are processes of the world, kept in `WorldActivity` with the rest: there is no separate body table. A body's owner is an entity, a fragment of a material, or a projectile carrying its striking surface (a damage type, an area, a give) and a tag the simulation gives it; a subgrid owner comes with subgrids. `Body` gains `plane`. `launch`, `impulse`, `steer` and `arrest` return a `Result`. `Body::of_mass` fills in a thing whose size the world is not told from its mass at 1000 kg/m³. What a body breaks has the body's doing as cause: a process, the entity it is as actor, the instigator of what launched it. | Design, from building bodies and releases | `WorldCtx::handle_wake` is the one entry point for the world's wake-ups (D-385), and a save that writes `WorldActivity` and the timeline must hold everything in flight. 07 §11.1 gives a projectile a surface, which the sketch's owner had no place for, and a bullet must not strike with the area drag acts on. The sketch could not refuse a body launched where nothing is loaded. | 02 §4, §15. `Cosmos::bodies`, `body`, `body_flight`, `body_of`. The simulation names a shot by `Projectile::tag`. |
| D-491 | A body sleeps for the time it takes to cross a cell at its speed, from 10 µs to 20 ms of local time, and when it wakes has flown for the time it slept: half of gravity's pull, thrust, drag in the frame of the moving medium by the closed form `w / (1 + k·|w|·dt)`, the other half of gravity, the turn toward the steered point; its position moves by the mean of the two velocities. The stretch is followed with the projectile ray and the first thing struck ends it. Ambient conditions are read where it wakes, and it goes onto the clock of the cell it is in after every wake and whenever an area's clock changes. Where nothing pulls on it and it is slower than `REST_SPEED` (0.5 m/s) it has stopped. | Design, from building bodies and releases | 02 §15.1 gave explicit Euler, which lands a two-second throw 0.2 m short and is unstable for a light body in air. This form is exact in vacuum for any step and cannot overshoot. Looking back over the time slept, never ahead, means nothing is struck before the body has reached it, so whoever has left a cell is not hit. Waking at ticks that are a function of its own state is what makes the result independent of how the timeline is advanced. A body adrift where there is no weight would otherwise be state that never ends (as D-384 for masses). | 02 §15.1, §15.3. An impact is dealt with up to one step late, and the rest of that step's travel is dropped. A body penetrates stopped time by at most one step. |
| D-492 | An impact is along the axis by which the path entered the cell; a floor, and anything come down onto inside a cell, is met from above or below. What closes part of a cell is struck by `share + √area / 1 m`, on a roll that is a positional hash of the body's own seed and the cell. A projectile delivers its surface's type at `KE / (area × give)`; anything else the kinetic type at `KE / (area × depth)`. A body that destroys a floor stays on the near side of it. An occupant flagged `COLLIDES` on a shared plane is struck as the path enters its cell: `BodyHitOccupant` carries a packet of the body's whole energy and the `push` the occupant takes, the body keeps `v·(m − e·M)/(m + M)`, and the world changes nothing else. An occupant that is a body is carried: moved by the world's `relocate` whenever the body enters another cell, reported as `OccupantDisplaced`. `BodyImpact` carries the element's `contact`. | Design, from building bodies and releases | 02 §15.2 said "the direction of entry", "a colliding occupant" and "conservation of momentum" without the rule for any, and 02 §22 left the roll for partial cover to whoever fires. A roll from a shared stream would depend on the order bodies wake in. A person is not a thin projectile: half a pillar should not miss them half the time. 07 §11.1 gives the hit the projectile's whole energy and lets the body model say what exits. The kernel already follows `OccupantDisplaced`, so someone in flight is where their body is with no new event, can be struck there (07 §7.1) and crosses areas as they pass. | 02 §7.4, §15, §15.2. The world knows no occupant's shape: whoever is in the cell is struck, once while the body passes. A shot that should pass through is given what exits by the simulation with `impulse`. |
| D-493 | An element destroyed by a packet with a direction and energy to spare throws up to three fragments: a quarter of the mass it lost and a hundredth of the energy left over, shared equally, each along the direction of the hit turned by up to 0.35 of a unit an axis by a positional hash of cell, tick and fragment. The fragments' energy is taken out of what passes and counted as absorbed. At most 256 fragments are in flight (`debris.max_bodies`); past that the element is left whole as rubble. Nothing is thrown by a blow with no direction, by rubble, by what fell or was crushed, or where what broke the element carries the wreck off. A fragment is a body on every plane and is left as rubble where it rests. | Design, from building bodies and releases | 02 §12.3 said "part of the element's mass" along "the direction of the hit" and gave no share, no number and no account of the energy. Handing the fragments the energy that also goes on with the body or the blast would count it twice (invariant W8). A falling mass takes the whole wreck of a floor down with it (D-385), so fragments there would be matter made from nothing. | 02 §12.3, §23. `DamageOutcome::passed` of a destruction that threw fragments is 99 hundredths of what the rule alone gives. The shares are named constants, settings when settings are bound. |
| D-494 | A body rests on what it came down onto and could not break once its speed back up is under `REST_SPEED`; resting, it slides, slowed by `friction × g`, and lands when it is slower than `REST_SPEED`. `friction` is a new field of `material`, a half where absent. A body that comes to space that is not loaded is finished by arithmetic: the same motion in 20 ms steps with nothing in the way, over ground taken to lie where it did under the last loaded cell. If a step ends in a loaded tract the body stays a body, `away` until it arrives; otherwise its impact is added to the tract's pending edits as `PendingEdit::Impact` and waits there, loading included, for the coarse layer. `BodyLeftLoaded` says where, when and whether it returns. A body's ticket has a fixed anchor it moves ahead of itself, onto at most three tracts that were not loaded. | Design, from building bodies and releases | 02 §15.3 named sliding friction and 02 §23 a `friction` no definition had. 02 §15.4 has the flight worked out "over the ground heights the tract records give", and the records hold none. Applying a pending impact is a release in resolve mode, which is the coarse layer's (W17). A `Body` ticket anchor would need the ticket table to know bodies; a fixed anchor moved by its holder is what the table already offers (D-376). | 02 §5.4, §10.1, §15.3, §15.4, §17.3, §23. A body beyond loaded space cannot be pushed or steered. An entity that does not return is the simulation's to take out and leave for that tract (06 §12). |
| D-495 | `release` returns a `Result` with the id of the release. A release's kind is any damage type; there is no definition type for releases. `speed` is optional: with none the front moves at the speed of sound where it starts, and in vacuum at `release.vacuum_speed` (1000 m/s). A beam says whether what is left of it bursts as a sphere where it ends. A release is a process of the world on the clock of its origin: shell `k` is the cells whose centres lie from `k` to `k + 1` metres from the origin point, handled storey by storey and row by row, one shell a wake, `1 m / speed` apart. `ReleaseStarted` is emitted when energy is let go and `EnergyReleased`, with the totals, when the front has gone as far as it goes. | Design, from building bodies and releases | The sketch of 02 §13 could not refuse a release where nothing is loaded and gave "defaults to the local sound speed" no way to be said. 02 §7.4 has `EnergyReleased` sum what a release did, which can only be known at the end, and presentation needs to know at the start. Shells by whole metres give the front a place on the timeline that does not depend on anything but the release. | 02 §7.4, §13, §23. `Cosmos::releases`. Fronts are W24's. |
| D-496 | The transmission map has six faces of `n × n` bins, `n = ⌈R·π/2⌉` from 16 to 48. A box covers the bins whose middles lie in the rectangle bounding it on each face, or else the one bin its middle lies in. A block or fixture covers its footprint up to its height and closes `coverage × 3 / height` of those bins; a floor covers a slab at the base of its cell and closes all of it. The shade of a shell falls when the shell is done. A floor receives `I × 3 m³` times the upward part of the direction to it; a floor above the origin is struck first and what it lets through is what the rest of its cell receives. No element is offered more than the release's energy times the share of all bins it covers, nor more than the release has left: the release keeps what is left of its energy and ends when nothing is. The share of a packet's spare energy that fragments carry is lowered from a tenth to a hundredth. | Design, from building bodies and releases | 02 §13.2 named a cube map and "the bins they cover" and nothing more. Bins shared by weight leak at every seam of a wall; covering by the middles of bins leaves no seam and keeps what is wholly behind a thing wholly in its shade (contract 2). Things of one shell stand side by side: letting one shade the next made a wall's cells shelter each other by the order they were visited in. `I × 3 m³ × coverage` summed over a shell is `3E / r`: within three metres the packets together offer more than was released, and the first thing struck was offered all of it. With a tenth, the turf round a car bomb left at a kilometre a second with tens of megajoules and broke walls the blast did not reach. | 02 §12.3, §13.2. Contract 1 holds exactly: `absorbed` is the energy less what was left. Bins are coarser than cells beyond about 30 m: a lone thing there shades a bin's width. |
| D-497 | Each occupant of a reached cell is exposed by the front once, with the cell's intensity and `I × 3 m³`. The wave gives a thing the energy it holds in a slab of the thing's cross-section one centimetre deep (`release.push_depth`), as speed straight away from the origin. A body in flight takes it as an impulse. An occupant takes the cross-section of a lump of its mass, and if the speed is at least 3 m/s (`release.throw_speed`) the world launches it as a body; slower, or with no mass told, or a device, it is left where it is. | Design, from building bodies and releases | 02 §13.2 gave an occupant "the same numbers" as an element, which has a coverage and an occupant has not, and left knockback to the simulation, which cannot push a thing between the shells of one release and whose launch threshold is combat's. The energy through a whole cell moved a clod of earth like a person; the energy through the thing's own cross-section gives a person about 5 m/s ten metres from half a tonne of explosive and next to nothing two metres from a grenade, which is the right order. | 02 §13.2, §23; 07 §7. The simulation must not launch again for an exposure the world has thrown for: `BodyLaunched` says it has. Staggering below the threshold stays the simulation's. |
| D-498 | What the front of a release cannot mark turns its packet back. When the front is done, if the space round the origin closes within 4000 cells by the fill that `enclose` uses, the energy turned back, and no more than is left, is held in it at `held / (cells × 3 m³)`: occupants in it are exposed once more and every fixture in it, block bounding it and floor over and under it receives a packet. A space that does not close holds nothing. A release whose next shell would pass `release.max_cells` (2,000,000 cells visited) is cut off there and says so in `EnergyReleased`. Cells that are not loaded are passed over. The three tiers, resolve mode, stamps and the ticket a release holds are not built. | Design, from building bodies and releases | 02 §13.2 had no confinement: a charge in a bunker did exactly what it does in a field, and a door made no difference. Holding the unspent energy in the closed volume is the least that makes a room matter, and uses the fill areas already have. Resolve mode needs the coarse layer's shelter factor and collapse without the timeline (W17, D-386); until then cutting a release off whole shells at a time is deterministic and said out loud. | 02 §13.2, §13.3, §13.4; plans/world-model.md W15, `beta`. A window that fails lets the room out: the front goes first, the fill is looked for after. |
| D-499 | Saved state names damage types and planes by save id, as it does materials: `DefKind` gains `damage_type` and `plane`. A body in flight has its fragment's material, its projectile's damage type and its planes turned, a release in progress its damage type, and a waiting impact what the body was. A set of planes is turned bit by bit; the mask of every plane is left as it is. A damage type that has gone is a placeholder whose default resistance nothing gets past; a plane that has gone is a placeholder on the next free bit, and a save that names more than thirty-two planes is refused. `launch` refuses a material or damage type that does not exist and drops a bit that names no plane. A release calls `conduct` once for each cell its front reaches, a beam for each cell it passes through and a closed space for each of its cells, with the cell's intensity and the energy the wave carries through a cell; a body calls it for the cell of the element or occupant it strikes, with the packet of the blow. The energy a release tells an occupant or a cell's fields is no more than the release let go. A world event is at most 176 bytes: `BodyHitOccupant`'s packet and `BodyImpact`'s contact are boxed, and `BodyHitOccupant`'s cause is its packet's. | Design, from fitting bodies and releases to saves, fields and the kernel | Bodies and releases were built beside persistence and fields. A dense id written as it is means another definition after the mods change, and a bit of a mask is a plane's id. A mask of every plane names no plane: turning it would make fragments of a wall miss whoever is on a plane added later. A save must not fail because a caller passed a mask with a bit nobody defined, and a kind of damage or a material nobody defined cannot be written at all. A front delivers one packet to a cell, however many elements share it: telling the fields once for each element would pass a shock on three times. Within half a metre `I × 3 m³` is more than the release itself. The kernel holds world events inline in enums beside small variants, which clippy refuses past a difference of 200 bytes; a blow's packet carried the body's cause twice. | 02 §11.2, §13.2, §15, §16; plans/world-model.md W14, W15, W16. With core's water a shock over a pond is passed on from every wet cell the front reaches: that is the reaction as written (D-504), and its cost is not bounded by `release.max_cells`. Field ids in tract blobs are still written as dense ids. |
| D-610 | Perception is a package of the simulation, `clash_sim::perception`, with five components (`senses`, `signature`, `awareness`, `beliefs`, `map_memory`) and one value on `Sim`, `PerceptionState` (signals in flight; the party's view, frames and tags), saved in the simulation's blob and hashed with the kernel. Its wake-ups are a domain of their own, `Perception`, added at the end of `WakeDomain`: a sweep's carries the observer and the sense, a delayed signal's arrival its id. The kernel's pump hands every event it delivers to `perception::observe_event`, after its handlers. Perception's events are engine events listed in `KERNEL_EVENTS` and raised as payloads of their types. Facing is degrees clockwise from north; an observer that moves on its grid turns to face the way it went. | Design, from building perception | 15 §4 and §5 named a `Senses` component and nothing else of where perception keeps what it knows. Every other kind of state the package keeps belongs to one entity, except signals on their way and the party's view, which belong to no one. Sharing the `Timer` domain would have needed one dispatcher for timers that other packages are wiring at the same time; the timeline's own comment says to add a domain at the end. A hook in the pump sees every event once, in delivery order, after a load as before, with nothing to register again. | 15 §3, §4, §5, §13; 03 §4.3. Additive in `clash-time` (`WakeDomain::Perception`). The save layout is version 4. `Sim::perception`, `Sim::perception_defs`, `Sim::party_perceived`. |
| D-611 | What holds a `through` signal back is a table on its signal kind, `block { default, materials, tags }`, each a share from 0 to 1: a named material first, then the largest share of the material's tags, then the default. The line from the source to the eye passes the blocks and fixtures of the cells strictly between them, each weighted by its coverage, and every floor it passes through between storeys, fully. Space that is not loaded passes nothing. | Design, from building perception | 15 §3.1 put `sound_block` on materials, with `block_by` naming another property for other kinds. That names a signal in the world crate, which gives materials no meaning, and makes a mod's new kind of signal edit every material. Tags carry it to materials a mod adds without either knowing the other. | 15 §3.1. `KindInfo::block` is worked out per material once. `mods/core` gives sound 0.3 by default and 0.8 for `mineral`. |
| D-612 | The `line` model's `exposed` is the share of four points up the source's height (`EXPOSURE_SAMPLES`) that a line of sight from the eye reaches; an eye is nine tenths of its owner's height up, or 1.5 m; a source with no height is 1.7 m tall. `light` is the light at the source's cell now, by the sky and the fixed sources. A field over its `opaque_from` in a cell strictly between hides the source. The `field` model reads the level over 255 in the observer's cell and perceives when it first reaches the threshold, from the neighbour on its storey with the most. A creature's standing signature of a kind it was not given is the kind's `standing`; anything else's is nothing; inside a container it is multiplied by the kind's `contained`, as is what reaches an observer inside one. `event_signal` gives events their signals; one is about nothing unless its `about` names a subject. | Design, from building perception | 15 §3.1 gave the formulas without saying how a share of a body is measured, from what height, or what a container does beyond sight. Items standing in view would otherwise each be detected and tracked. Breaking glass tells where, not who. | 15 §3.1, §3.2, §5.3. `signal::strength_at`, `exposure`, `passing`, `fields_hide`, `bound_at`. |
| D-613 | A delayed signal travels at its kind's `speed`, or the speed of sound of the atmosphere where it starts, and in vacuum goes nowhere. It is sent to each observer within its kind's reach (the furthest a sense of it reaches) when given off, on the clock of where it starts. When its front reaches where an observer stood, the observer receives it if it is there or nearer; if it has moved off, the front follows it while it is within its sense's range. | Design, from building perception | 15 §3.1 said only `distance / speed`. A wave whose every cell were followed would cost a process per signal; a reception fixed at emission would let someone outrun sound and still hear it. Following only the observers in reach is what bounds the work. | 15 §3.1. Test script `slow_sound` (S2). An observer who comes into reach after a signal was given off does not hear it. |
| D-614 | Awareness builds by 15 §5.1 with ε = 0.1; `dt` is the time since the sense last swept, and a signal given off counts as standing for one interval. A sense sweeps at its interval, its first sweep at a point drawn from a positional hash of the observer and the sense; an observer that moves sweeps at once if a quarter of the interval has passed; a target's own move is caught by the next sweep. A sweep considers at most `perception.max_candidates` (32) of those in range, nearest first, and every target already tracked through the sense; a target whose signal could not reach the threshold with nothing in the way costs no line of sight. An observer keeps at most `perception.max_awareness` (128) pairs. A warm observer does not sweep and receives only signals over `perception.warm_factor` times its threshold. | Design, from building perception | 15 §5.2 listed triggers without bounding them: re-evaluating every observer of a target at each of its steps costs the square of a crowd for every step. Staggering by hash keeps P4 and spreads the work. Without a cap on candidates a crowd churned: pairs dropped for room were detected again at the next sweep. | 15 §5.1, §5.2, §14, §17. Measured: 23 ms a simulated second for fifty creatures in one street, over the target of 15 §17. |
| D-615 | `perceived` binds `self` and, when made out, `about`; its fields are `percept`, `sense`, `signal`, `event`, `about_actor`, `where` and `clarity`, with ids qualified. An observer makes out what a signal is about, or the actor of the event it carries, only if it has them detected already or the kind `identifies` and they stand where the signal came from. Perception's events carry a cause with no actor. `perceives` and `could_perceive` are functions of truth for world rules and windows; a mind asks properties of the same names of itself. Whoever builds windows asks `notices_source(candidate, source)` first. | Design, from building perception | 15 §6 said the true actor is not in a percept, but every event binds `actor` from its cause. A sound tells where, not who. A function cannot tell whose view it answers for, so the mind's own form is a property of its own subject. | 15 §6; 03 §7.2. Test script `heard_not_seen` (S4): `tests/fixtures/perception/heard_not_seen.json` holds the rule for when `enqueue_action` exists. |
| D-616 | Beliefs are kept by persona. Until personas (S6), every entity presents one persona whose id is its own (`PersonaId`). The belief properties of 15 §7.2, and `last_seen_at`, `seen_how`, `perceives`, `could_perceive`, `remembers` and `knows_element`, are properties of an `entity` read of the mind itself: none is marked safe for belief, because read of another entity it would tell one mind what another believes, and a mind context may read anything of its own subject. `knows` and `perceives` are false for the never-perceived, the rest null. Confidence halves every `mind.confidence_halflife` (60 s) and salience every `mind.salience_halflife` (600 s); tracks, memories, noted things and observed capabilities are bounded by settings, weakest and oldest first to go. | Design, from building perception | 15 §7.2 wrote `self.knows(x)` and left open whether these are marked safe. Marked safe, `target.believed_loc(self)` would compile in a mind context. A persona that is the entity for now keeps invariant P2 in the storage, so S6 changes only how an entity is translated. | 15 §7; 05 §7.3; 09 §3.4. `belief::track_of`, `believed_loc`, `confidence`, `add_track`, `clear_track`, `remember`, `note_element`, `observe_capability`. |
| D-617 | The linter compiles the condition of every rule attached to an entity again in the mind context, `self` as its own subject, and reports what that refuses as unsafe for belief as a warning on the rule. The loader gives a cross check the registry its definitions were compiled against (`DefsView::expr_registry`). Truth properties the kernel withheld from minds (D-466) are not relaxed: their believed counterparts are the mind's own properties; a cell's shape and `los` between cells may be marked safe once their getters take an element tagged `hidden` for what it appears to be. | Design, from building perception | 15 §7.3 asked the linter to flag direct reads of another entity's state. The compiler's own belief check already says what a mind may read; a second, weaker list would drift from it. A cross check had no registry to compile with. | 15 §7.3; 05 §5.4. Additive in `clash-data`. Effect arguments are not checked yet. |
| D-618 | The party is every directly controlled entity with senses. A member observes at each sweep of a sense of a kind that uses light: the cells in its field of view within range and arc, one storey up to three down, lit enough for a creature standing there to be seen. What it sees goes into its `MapMemory` (slabs of a storey of a tract, at most `perception.map_slabs_max`) and into a queued `PartyFrame`: cells that came into view or changed, cells that left the party's view, entities in view, what was heard. Every delivered event is tagged `Seen`, `Heard { from }` or `Not`; `Sim::party_perceived` answers for the last 8192. `perception.omniscient` makes every event seen. Frames and tags are saved and not hashed. | Design, from building perception | 15 §13 said what is recorded and not how the view model is fed or how playback asks. The scene's view model takes cells one at a time and keeps no handle on the world (D-350), so the simulation hands over what changed. Looking up through the open sky put a hundred thousand cells in a view; the storeys one plays on are what is drawn. | 15 §13; 12 §5. `perception::drain_frames`, `snapshot`, `party_view`, `set_omniscient`. Moving lights are not counted yet. |
| D-619 | Paths over belief are movement rules that wrap the mover's own: `believed_move_rules_with(sim, mind, rules)`, or `believed_move_rules(sim, mind)` over the walker that opens doors. Something hidden that the mind has not noted, which would have to be opened to pass, is taken for a wall. A mind with map memory plans through cells it has never seen at 1.5 times the cost (`UNKNOWN_CELL_FACTOR`). | Design, from building perception | 15 §7.3 makes static geometry common knowledge but for what is hidden; 09 §5.3 has doors and hazards cost by what is believed. Wrapping keeps what a body can do apart from what its mind knows, so movement need not change to use it. | 15 §7.3; 09 §5. Movement switches by building its rules for a character and passing them through `believed_move_rules_with`. Noted locks and hazards wait for devices and fields. |
| D-540 | The rule runtime is bound to the simulation by a host over the simulation itself, made for one run (`Sim::rule_host`), which reads truth, draws from the rule stream and writes through `Sim::set_var` and `Sim::set_property`. The rules of the definitions are a `RuleSet` kept beside the state, with a table of verbs by registry id built from `engine_verbs()`. One bus handler for each event type some rule listens for hands the event over and writes back changed fields, `consume` and `cancel`. What a rule changes carries a cause of kind `eoc`: its actor is the rule's owner, else `self`, else the actor of the event answered; its instigator is the subject `instigator`, else that of the event, else the actor. `emit` raises its event with that cause and stamps it on the clock of the first entity it binds. Faults, and run-time warnings of expressions rules evaluate, are `fault` events thinned by count; the counts are not state. `RuleHost` gained `writing`, by which the interpreter says what is running before a write. | Design, from binding the rule runtime | D-470 left the binding as six steps and the interface gave a write no way to say who caused it, so a watched variable set by a rule would have raised `var_changed` with nobody behind it. A host over `&mut Sim` lets a verb be a plain function that calls the engine's own functions (05 E2), and a hook a rule raises is delivered through the same bus and runs rules again. Counts that thin reports decide nothing, and the output is not hashed, so keeping them out of the save changes no hash. | 05 §13.1; 03 §4.1. `clash_sim::rule::SimRules`, `Sim::run_effects`, `Sim::call_rule`, `Sim::rule_set`. The reasons `rule.verb_unbound` and `rule.expression_warning`. The save layout is version 4, and version 5 once joined with the stores of items (D-569) and the action table (D-543). Rules carried by definitions (`rules_of`) wait for a definition type of the simulation that carries `rules`. |
| D-541 | The kernel's verbs are `set_time_rate { who, rate? }`, `anchor_clock { who, to? }`, `set_clock_rate { of, rate? }`, `set_control { who, mode }`, `add_tag { to, tag }` and `remove_tag { from, tag }`, each a call of the kernel function of that purpose. A tag given or taken raises `tag_changed`. A verb given an argument it cannot use declines with `verb.bad_argument`. The events of actions, plans, free decisions, messages, choices and tags are one variant of the event body, `Act`, holding an enum of their own with its own table, as the world's are. | Design, from binding the rule runtime | 03 §13 named the three clock effects and `set_control` without arguments, and nothing let a rule change a tag although every rule may test one. An event for each package added to five matches in one file is five merges for each package; one variant is one. | 03 §13. `clash_sim::kernel_verbs`, `Sim::set_tag`, `clash_sim::action::ActEvent`, `ACT_EVENTS`. `destroy`, `teleport` and the verbs of bodies, items and movement are their packages'. |
| D-542 | `message` and `ask` are effects of the rule system, read by its compiler, not verbs. `message` raises the event `message` about its reader with the text rendered for that reader. `ask` asks a directly controlled entity through a `Choice` decision answered by the `Choose` command, and keeps the choice in the rule state as plain data: where the `ask` is written (definition type, qualified id and JSON path), the subjects, locals and event fields bound there, and the cause. Anyone else takes the first option whose `if` held, at once. Either way `choice_made` is raised before the option runs. A choice whose chooser stops being directly controlled is settled then; one whose chooser is removed is forgotten. What a reader knows of others is a reader lens the simulation holds, the truth until perception exists; the English forms are built in until the `language` type exists. | Design, from building messages and choices (K9) | A verb's argument is one value of a declared type: it cannot be a template rendered for a reader, nor a list of effects to run once someone answers. A choice that waits across a save cannot hold the compiled tree; the place it is written at names it again after a load, and fails with a reason if the definitions changed under the save. | 05 §4.6, §10.6, §13.2; 03 §4.4, §5.2, §13. `clash_sim::reader`, `rule::{Ask, PendingChoice, EffectSite, EffectRoots}`, `RuleHost::{set_reader, message, offer}`, `RuleSet::resume_choice`, `Command::Choose`. The reasons `rule.choice_refused`, `rule.choice_lost`, `rule.message_refused`. A choice's option runs on a copy of the locals: an `ask` in an action's list cannot change the running action's own. |
| D-543 | The action runtime keeps a running action as plain state in the action table: actor, definition, one stored value for each local in slot order, phase, slots held by channel, the wake-ups of its phase and its tick, when the phase began and is due, and the count of phases entered in the current tick. Starting checks the actor, the definition, the parameters (their types, list bounds and defaults, in order), roles, `footing`, `requires` and slots, in that order, changing nothing on a refusal (the random streams are put back); then `action_starting` if anything listens, the claim, `action_started`, `requires_at_start`, `on_start` and the first phase. A refusal by a handler is raised as `action_refused`, and the command that asked is taken. An entity's slots of a channel are its body's, or one of every channel without a body, less those disabled; slots are counted, not named. Slots held by an action whose phase allows cancelling for the new action count as free and that action is cancelled. Entering a phase schedules its end before its `do` runs, so that `phase_entered` carries the due tick and precedes what the `do` raises. Leaving the world, by any path, abandons an entity's actions. | Design, from building the action runtime (T3) | 03 §6 gave the steps and left their order, the default slots of an entity with no body, what a `cancel` list is for and what a veto after the handlers ran does to a command open. A refused command is not logged, so a refusal after handlers have changed state would replay differently: it must be taken. A `cancel` list of tags only means something if starting a tagged action can break off the phase. | 03 §4.4, §6.6, §13. `clash_sim::action::{run, state}`, `Sim::start_action`, `cancel_action`, `interrupt_action`, `check_slots`, `slots_of`, `enabled_slots`, `held_slots`; `Command::Act`, `Cancel`. The reasons `action.unknown`, `action.not_running`, `action.interrupted`, `action.bad_time`. A `part` parameter does not name a slot (`engine:action_slot_unavailable` is not given yet); a joint action claims only its first role's slots (T6). |
| D-544 | An entity is free when it is in the world, no foreground action of its own runs, its plan is empty and it is not waiting. When it becomes free its plan goes on first; otherwise a directly controlled entity is asked with one `Free` request, and anyone else has `decision_needed` raised for it. It becomes free on coming under direct control or entering the world under it (`started`), when a foreground action finishes or is cancelled, is interrupted, when a plan step cannot start, and when a wait ends. An entity not under direct control that nothing gives anything to do idles for `actions.idle_wait` and is asked again, but only while something listens for `decision_needed`; entering the world without direct control asks nothing. Commands that act address entities under direct or delegated control only. A wait of no length answers without asking again. A plan step waits for busy slots, and any other refusal clears the plan and asks with the reason. | Design, from building control and plans (T4) | Asking every entity that enters the world would ask items; idling with nobody listening would fill the timeline with wake-ups nobody answers and keep `UntilDecision` from ever returning. A frontend needs a way to answer a free decision without acting and without being asked again at once. | 03 §4.4, §5. `clash_sim::action::plan`, `Sim::is_free`, `wait`, `enqueue_action`, `clear_plan`; `Command::Wait`, `ClearPlan`; the setting `actions.idle_wait`; the reasons `command.not_controlled`, `command.bad_argument`. The AI layer (09) answers `decision_needed`. |
| D-545 | The verbs of actions and plans are `start_action { who, action, with? }`, which gives whether the action started and raises `action_refused` rather than failing when it cannot; `enqueue_action { who, action, with? }`; `cancel_action { who, action?, channel?, tag? }` and `interrupt { who, action?, channel?, tag? }`, which pick the running actions that match all that is named and give how many they ended; `clear_plan { who }`; `interrupt_plan { who }`; `disable_channel { who, channel, count? }` and `enable_channel { who, channel, count? }`; and `wait { who, time }`. | Design, from building the action runtime (T3, T4) | 03 §13 named the effects without arguments. An action that cannot start is an outcome rules must expect, not a fault of the rule. | 03 §13. `clash_sim::action::verbs`. The verbs of windows, reactions and roles are packages T5 and T6's. |
| D-560 | How a thing is held is a component of the holder, `Holds`: what it holds other than `Inside`, in order of id, each with its hold, and the tags it carries only because of what it holds. A grasp is the slots it occupies, by part and channel, among the parts that work (03 §6.2); worn is each part covered with its layer and the `wear` entry that covers it. Containment stays the kernel's `Place::In`, and every move goes through `relocate`, which tells the container an entity left so that it forgets the record. | Design, from building the beta | 06 §9.2 named `Grasp(part)` and `Worn(parts, layer)` without saying where they live. On the item they would duplicate the place and could disagree with it; on the holder, one call from `relocate` keeps them true whoever moves the thing, which is invariant B6 for holds. A two-handed grip needs slots on more than one part, and one item may lie at different layers over different parts. | 06 §9.2; package E8. `Sim::relocate` calls `item::left_holder`. A record never names what is not inside its holder, and what an item confers ends exactly when it leaves. |
| D-561 | What an item confers is two fields of the `item` type beside `grants`, `traits` and `rules`: `modifiers` (`attr`, `add`, `mul`), added to the holder's stats under the item as their source (06 §4.1), and `holder_tags`. They apply while it is in a grasp or worn, not while it is stowed. A tag the holder already had is not counted as conferred, and one two items confer stays until neither does. | Design, from building the beta | The brief asked for attribute modifiers through stats and tags; 06 §9.1 had only `grants`, `traits` and `rules`, which wait for packages not built. An item is its data: there is no armour class or damage bonus, only what a modder writes as modifiers. | 06 §9.1; package E8. What a holder has from its items always equals `item::conferred`, which a property test checks under random holding. Until the kernel joins the stats, the tables are built for each change. |
| D-562 | Two items are equal, and may share an entity with a count, when they were made from the same definition that stacks and agree in material, damage, colour, tags, variables and identity, and neither holds anything or has latent contents. A container does not stack. A member is split off before it is damaged or worn; a piece split from a grasped stack shares its grasp; equal items in the same place and the same kind of hold rejoin after a drop, a `put_in` and a repair, and taking more of a stack already in hand joins it without another hand. | Design, from building the beta | 06 §9.1 stated the rule without the details that make it buildable. Sharing slots lets an archer hold a quiver's worth of arrows in one hand, and keeps counts and mass conserved through every split and merge. | 06 §9.1, §12.2; package E8. A pile of forty arrows is one entity. Notable members are those split off; nothing more is kept for an anonymous member. |
| D-563 | An item's capacity is its material's toughness times one member's volume, and its damage is counted in 255ths of it by the element rule (02 §12.2). At 255 it is broken: still there and held as it was, but no longer a layer when worn. What a worn layer absorbed of a hit becomes the 255ths of the item's whole capacity that the joules amount to, at least one; a layer that was not breached stops at 254. | Design, from building the beta | The body model resolves a worn layer against the stuff under the contact (D-524) and reports joules; the item keeps one damage for all of it, as an element does, so that a coat worn through on one arm is a worn coat. | 06 §7.2, §9.1; package E8. `item::apply_worn_hits` takes `HitReport.worn`; `item::worn_layers_of` hands a hit every layer, outermost first over each part. |
| D-564 | A container's latent contents come from the `contents` list of its own definition (`item`, `count` as a number or `[least, most]`, `chance`), drawn in order from a generator seeded by the container's seed alone with every draw made whether or not it matters. The seed is fixed when it is made: from the stream `items`, or given by generation. `materialise_contents`, putting something in, and the first packet that affects it open it; what the list gives is stowed whether or not it fits. `content_table` definitions are not built. | Design, from building the beta | 06 §9.3 asks for the same items whenever they are first made; a seed of the container's own, and draws that do not depend on each other's outcome, give that in any order of opening and across a save. | 06 §9.3; package E8. Shared tables of contents wait for generation of sites (WG8). |
| D-565 | A part that comes off a body is an item of no definition: `Item.def` is null and it keeps the part as it was (`remains`, the body's `Detached`). It is made of the material of the root part's thickest layer, has the part's mass and the volume of its tissues, carries the tags of the part's definition, lies where the body is, and its `Identity` names the `body_part` definition. What the body wore only on the parts that came off goes inside it; what it wore on other parts too stays on those; what the parts grasped falls.
